## Where #545 stands

#545 turned five host-killing arms of the DC21285 footbridge (`dev_footbridge.c`) into decline + once-latched
announce.  Its offline detector `regress/diff_footbridge.c` #includes the device and drives the access function
directly (rows counted by name; the gate pins the counts).  Repair 3 (862 rows) -> its pass -> **3c (864: nine X14
cells the Opus seat measured open, two rows added)** -> **3c's pass**: `panel_20260923_192724` (codex, grok and glm DO
NOT SHIP; agy, deepseek, minimax and kimi-k3:cloud SHIP; the kimi CLI walled on its weekly limit, covered by
kimi-k3:cloud), a dedicated codex job (FAIL), and the Opus measuring seat (DO NOT SHIP: 8 edits green on 864, 7 of
them closable by scratch rows).  **Four seats independently: the new "Clear 0x20 keeps 0x10 pending" row names the
pending status and never reads it.**  -> **THIS: repair 3d.**

**The chain rule this pass is judged by** (the carrier's repair-chain rule): one pass per floor-bound diff; the chain
closes on the first VALID pass that finds ZERO floor breaches -- the diff delivered, the roster named, and the zero
carried with its denominator.  Review EFFORT is scoped to 3d's diff; the FLOOR QUESTION (a clean-sweep mutant in the
DEPENDS-ON enumeration, or a claimed cell a measured edit passes) is asked of the SHIPPED COMPOSITE.

## What 3d changes (the full diff is inlined below) -- NO ROW ADDED; 864 rows, X14's 21 names

* **E1** the DEVINIT "Clear 0x20 keeps 0x10 pending and enabled" rows (both timer states) now read `irq_status == 0x18`
  -- the raw status a Clear must not touch.
* **E2** the line-4 "IRQ_STATUS reads 0x10" row now reads the FIQ fields too (`fiq_status == 0 && fiq_enable == 0`),
  so its COVERED claim "(value, fields, output)" names all five fields it reads.
* **E3** both asserted-Clear rows ("line 4 raised by a device: Clear 0x10 clears it / Clear 0x400 keeps it") now read
  the raw status (`irq_status == 0x10`) and are RENAMED "..., the raw status kept, ...".  `irq_asserted` is left
  UNPINNED there on purpose: the enable arms never write it (`fbirqstuck`'s F13 defect), so pinning 1 after a
  deasserting Clear pins the defect and pinning 0 is false of today's code.
* **E4** the re-arm row now reads `irq_asserted == 1` and the FIQ fields at its end.
* **E5** X12 and X13 fill the buffer with 0xa5 before each narrow Clear write, so the bytes above the width are not
  zero (a real store's are not): a write-data fetch that reads past `len` now clears more of the enable.
* Records: COVERED (the reads' widths spelled out -- IRQEnable's 8-byte read NOT covered; "for a read, the read data";
  X14's channels), NOT COVERED (the exact-value example rewritten from the Opus seat's measured forms; bit 16 of the
  unraised 25 is caught; the producer pair's text and its filing by owner ruling; NEW: the CALLER context, the announce
  TEXT, a latch's HISTORY; FS02 on the LEVEL line; the rest of the instance), the X14 header, the IDENTITY comment.

## AC �?" SETTLED PREMISES (recorded resolutions; say if the diff or the handler contradicts one)

1. **The product change is settled** -- decline + once-latched announce at the five sites, 0 for the declined read.
   No repair touches `dev_footbridge.c`.
2. **The floor** (the carrier's DEPENDS-ON rule, bounded): the predicates DOMINATING each edited decline, walked to
   the function boundary, crossed with the body-read lvalues' BODY-TESTED partitions where jointly reachable; a value
   or combination the bodies never test as a case is DISCLOSED (clause e), not rowed.  The contract may claim MORE
   than the floor; a claimed cell that a measured edit passes is then a breach of the contract, and repair 3 rows it
   (or narrows the claim) rather than arguing it is above the floor.
3. **Insertion-class edits in OTHER cases** (the +0x188 Set arm, the FIQ enable pair, TIMER_1_VALUE) are outside the
   closure; their existing rows stay, ungraded as floor.  `fbenablewidth` (narrow Set/FIQ writes) is filed.
4. **`fiq_status` has NO writer** in the product: every FIQ status value the rows seed is synthetic.  **`irq_status`
   and `irq_asserted` are written ONLY by `footbridge_interrupt_assert` / `_deassert`** (below).
5. **Width 8 reaches the 8-byte-aligned offsets only** (`cpu_arm_instr_loadstore.c`: `addr &= ~(datalen - 1)` before
   one `memory_rw` of len 8; `memory_rw` passes len through): of the five decline sites +0x180 and +0x280, and the
   enable arms +0x188 and +0x288.  An 8-byte access to +0x184, +0x18c or +0x284 is not a guest input.
6. **The lines a device raises**: DEVINIT connects the four timers to lines 4-7; `bus_pci.c` routes cats ISA to line
   10 (`"%s.10.isa"`), netwinder ISA to 11 (`irq_path_isa` `.11`), and the cats dec21143 to 18 (`irq = 18`).  No
   device raises the other 25 lines, and a guest cannot set a bit of `irq_status`.
7. **DEVINIT's timer reset values**: `timer_control[i] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC` (0x40), `timer_load[i] = TIMER_MAX_VAL`
   (0x00FFFFFF), `pending_bound[i] = 1`; a calloc'd instance has all three at 0.
8. **The producer pair is DISCLOSED and FILED, not rowed** -- owner ruling 2026-09-23 (under `fbirqstuck`, MED).
9. **The fork's `fatal()` returns**; the detector stubs it and counts calls.


## The handler -- `DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)`, the parts this closure is about (verbatim, frozen copy)

NOT SHOWN: the PCI-config, UART and timer arms between `switch (relative_addr) {` and `case IRQ_STATUS:`, and the
timer arms after `case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:`.  They read and write no field the five arms read, EXCEPT that a timer
tick reaches `irq_status` through the interrupt callbacks (see AC.5).

```c
DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)
{
	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	int timer_nr = 0;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	if (relative_addr >= TIMER_1_LOAD && relative_addr <= TIMER_4_CLEAR) {
		timer_nr = (relative_addr >> 5) & (N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS - 1);
		relative_addr &= ~0x060;
	}

	switch (relative_addr) {
	/* ... NOT SHOWN: VENDOR_ID, DEVICE_ID, PCI config, UART ... */

	case IRQ_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->irq_status & d->irq_enable;
		else if (!d->warned_irq_status_w) {
			d->warned_irq_status_w = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to irq status? "
			    "(once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_RAW_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->irq_status;
		else if (!d->warned_irq_raw_w) {
			d->warned_irq_raw_w = 1;
			fatal("[ footbridge write to irq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_ENABLE_SET:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->irq_enable |= idata;
			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
			else
				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
		} else {
			odata = d->irq_enable;
		}
		break;

	case IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			d->irq_enable &= ~idata;
			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
			else
				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
		} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {
			d->warned_irq_clear_r = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge read from "
			    "ENABLE CLEAR? (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->fiq_status & d->fiq_enable;
		else if (!d->warned_fiq_status_w) {
			d->warned_fiq_status_w = 1;
			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to fiq status? "
			    "(once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_RAW_STATUS:
		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
			odata = d->fiq_status;
		else if (!d->warned_fiq_raw_w) {
			d->warned_fiq_raw_w = 1;
			fatal("[ footbridge write to fiq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
		}
		break;

	case FIQ_ENABLE_SET:
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->fiq_enable |= idata;
		break;

	case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
		if (wstatus & d->irq_enable) && d->irq_asserted) {
		d->irq_asserted = 0;
		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
	}
}
```

## AF2 �?" 3c's pass findings, with MY disposition of each (the panel sharing its findings)

Every seat answered blind to the others.  Here is each finding, who raised it, and what I did.  **Adjudicate your own
findings harder than anyone else's**, and say whether the FIX is right, not only whether the finding was.

* **S1** [codex, grok, glm, codex-dedicated; the Opus seat's FS01 is its asserted twin] the Clear 0x20 row names the
  pending status and never reads it; a Clear zeroing it at DEVINIT values passed 864.  **ACCEPTED -> E1** (CXC1 green
  -> red x2).
* **S2** [grok; glm secondary; codex-dedicated for the re-arm row] the IRQ_STATUS read row claims "(value, fields,
  output)" and never read the FIQ fields.  **ACCEPTED -> E2 + E4** (GF1 green -> red x2).
* **S3** [the Opus seat, FS01] a Clear dropping the masked line from the RAW status while irq_asserted -- guest-visible
  in 4 steps -- passed; the asserted-Clear rows named only the enable and the CPU line.  **ACCEPTED -> E3**, zero rows
  (FS01 green -> red x1).  `irq_asserted` deliberately unpinned there (E3's reason).
* **S4** [agy; codex-dedicated] the re-arm row's final check omitted `irq_asserted` (and the FIQ fields).  **ACCEPTED ->
  E4** (a decline clearing irq_asserted, DA1: green -> red x1).
* **S5** [deepseek] a fetch of 4 bytes whatever `len` is passed: every narrow write left the bytes above its width 0.
  **ACCEPTED -> E5** (FW1 green -> red x16).  The fetch is NOT an edited line, so this is above the floor; I took it
  because the fill costs no row and the delta is measured (the addition licence).  **A seat may say that is wrong.**
* **S6** [the Opus seat, MC01-MC05, MC07] every row calls the handler with mem NULL on a machine of type MACHINE_NONE,
  so a condition on `mem` or on the machine type passes.  **DISCLOSED, clause (e)**: no body of the five reads `mem` or
  the machine type (the joint-cells rule: a selector that touches nothing the guarded body reads is (e)).  Filed with
  the Opus seat's scratch rows (a cats and a netwinder caller with a real mem: 871 rows, 8 of its 9 greens red) as the
  ready detector.  Measured green on 3d, 6 of 6.
* **S7** [the Opus seat TX01; kimi-k3:cloud] the announce TEXT is unread.  **DISCLOSED.**
* **S8** [codex-dedicated] a latch that re-arms after four repeats passes (no instance declines one site five times).
  **DISCLOSED, clause (e)**: the latch test partitions {0, nonzero}, and both are rowed.  LH1 green on both, measured.
* **S9** [the Opus seat FS02] a Clear clearing irq_asserted passes.  **DISCLOSED** on the LEVEL line.
* **S10** [grok, the Opus seat] records: "X14 calls ... only to BUILD its instances" was false (PR09 reds the
  status-read row); "That pair is fbirqstuck's round" conflated F13's stuck-high line with the pair's bit logic
  (PR01/PR03/PR04); "passes all 862 rows" and F1's "red on the 862 rows" were stale.  **ACCEPTED -> rewritten.**
* **S11** [codex, the Opus seat] COVERED's reads line claimed IRQEnable x width 8 while NOT COVERED named it.
  **ACCEPTED** -> widths spelled out.
* **S12** [the Opus seat, codex] the exact-value example undercounted (the irq_* `!= 0x10` forms are red at +0x184
  since 3c and at all five latch tests; every fiq_* form is green) and `idata != 0x20` is red since 3c, not since X14;
  [minimax] "by value, not by design" was ambiguous.  **ACCEPTED** -> rewritten from the measured forms.
* **S13** [codex] bit 16 of the 25 unraised bits IS caught (N_SB16 reds ten rows: one each in X9/X10, four each in
  X12/X13); 24 masks pass.  **ACCEPTED.**
* **S14** [grok] NOT COVERED's field list omitted the rest of the instance THE SPACE names.  **ACCEPTED.**
* **S15** [glm] the X14 header's recap of the nine did not name TD04 (a decline driving at DEVINIT values, NOT
  asserted).  **ACCEPTED.**
* **S16** [kimi-k3:cloud] COVERED's "the read data over the width" read as a channel of the write rows.  **ACCEPTED** ->
  "for a read".
* **S17** [codex-dedicated] three PRE-EXISTING false comments (fbclearread's `case TIMER_1_CLEAR` sentence, "EVERY
  REAL ROW IN THIS FILE COMPARES NUMBERS", the DISABLED-timer sentence also in gate_offline.sh).  **FILED with R17**
  (pre-existing, the timer campaign's), not edited here.
* **S18** [R4 options: codex, grok, agy, minimax, kimi-k3:cloud, the Opus seat] a one-row len-1 Clear on X14's split
  counters, or re-wiring X9-X13's `d->irq` to X14's CPU counter pair (zero rows), would close the destination gap.
  **KEPT DISCLOSED** (the Clear write's body is not an edited line); both options go into the filing.
* **S19** [R2 options: kimi-k3:cloud, minimax, codex, grok, agy, the Opus seat] a bounded producer block.  **OWNER
  RULING 2026-09-23: disclose + file under `fbirqstuck`** (MED); the options and the Opus seat's ready rows go there,
  with the pair's bit logic named apart from F13 (grok).

  the carrier records this).  No change.
* **S21** [kimi-k3:cloud] the family pins sum to 831, not 864: the other 33 are the 32 timer rows and IDENTITY
  (codex and grok enumerated 864).  No change.
* **S22** [agy, Q2] `(len & 1) == 0` on the Clear direction test: len 1 is odd, so X12's len-1 rows red it; the
  `len == 3` variant is not a guest input.  Not an escape.

**DISAGREEMENTS -- both sides, in their own words:**
* **D1** kimi-k3:cloud: *"'keeps 0x10 pending' is observed via the drive, exactly."*  codex, grok, glm: *"The drive
  proves bit 4 was live at drive time, not that the field retains it afterwards."*  **Settled by measurement**: CXC1
  zeroes the status AFTER the drive and passed all 864 rows on 3c.
* **D2** agy: *"No X14 row claims a channel it does not read."*  codex, grok, glm: the Clear 0x20 row and the status-read
  row do.  **Settled by CXC1 and GF1.**
* **D3** kimi-k3:cloud: *"disclosure + file is right iff fbirqstuck is a real, owned round."*  The other seats upheld
  R2 on the DEPENDS-ON function-boundary rule.  `fbirqstuck` is an open MED item and the owner ruled.
* **D4 (mine, open): E3 and E5 are rows/fills ABOVE the floor** -- the Clear write's body and the head fetch are not
  edited lines.  I took both because each costs no row and each has a measured delta.  The alternative is to disclose
  them, as S6/S8 are disclosed.  Which is right, and does taking them change what the CONTRACT claims (COVERED now
  names the raw status at the asserted Clears)?

## AE -- QUESTIONS (answer each, numbered; if you are answering from this packet alone, do Q1 and Q3 first)

**Q1 -- did 3d close what it claims?**  (a) For each strengthened check (E1-E4) and the fill (E5), name the edit it is
for and the observable that now differs -- and say whether ANY row still claims a channel it does not read.  (b) Is
every COVERED line true of the 864 rows, and every NOT COVERED line outside the floor or honestly disclosed?
**Q2 -- THE FLOOR QUESTION, on the composite**: the smallest edit to the five arms, the two head tests, or the Clear
write inside the edited case, that the 864 rows cannot see and the contract does not disclose.  Name it and the rows you
expect to stay green; **if none, give your denominator** -- a bare zero does not close the chain.
**Q3 -- adjudicate S1-S22 and D1-D4**: UPHOLD / OVERTURN / REFINE / CANNOT SETTLE, one line each.  Then: **WHICH OF MY
ACCEPTED DISPOSITIONS IS WRONG?**  Above all D4 and S6 (caller context disclosed rather than rowed).
**Q4 -- records.**  Is any sentence in the changed comments false as of today?  Check the two renamed row names against
their checks, and the IDENTITY comment's history.
**Q5 -- closing.**  Is this pass VALID to close the chain if it finds zero breaches (diff delivered, roster, your
denominator)?  If you would not close, name the one measurement that would let you.

If, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE -- say so.

End with a seat record: model, what you read, what you ran.


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

100:## The handler -- `DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)`, the parts this closure is about (verbatim, frozen copy)
101:
102:NOT SHOWN: the PCI-config, UART and timer arms between `switch (relative_addr) {` and `case IRQ_STATUS:`, and the
103:timer arms after `case FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:`.  They read and write no field the five arms read, EXCEPT that a timer
104:tick reaches `irq_status` through the interrupt callbacks (see §C.5).
105:
106:```c
107:DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)
108:{
109:	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
110:	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
111:	int timer_nr = 0;

220:### The producer -- the only writers of `irq_status` and `irq_asserted` (verbatim, frozen `dev_footbridge.c`)
221:
222:```c
223:void footbridge_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
224:{
225:	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;

 succeeded in 432ms:
226:	d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;
227:
228:	// Patch from NetBSD:
229:	// "fix hang interrupt issue. Always assert the irq's even if we have
230:	//  already asserted the global irq for the device. prevents hangs with
231:	//  cats."
232:	if ((d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) /* && !d->irq_asserted */) {
233:		d->irq_asserted = 1;
234:		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
235:	}
236:}
237:
238:void footbridge_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
239:{
240:	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
241:	d->irq_status &= ~interrupt->line;
242:
243:	if (!(d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) && d->irq_asserted) {
244:		d->irq_asserted = 0;
245:		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
246:	}
247:}

/*
 *  Offline differential for the SHIPPED footbridge timer -- src/devices/dev_footbridge.c.
 *
 *  WHY THIS EXISTS.  #432 fixed a guest-reachable HOST CRASH: `random() % timer_load[i]` with
 *  a guest-writable zero load is an integer division by zero, reproduced independently by two
 *  seats as SIGFPE (exit 136).  A crash fix with no detector is this project's fifth vacuity
 *  class, and here the detector had to be offline for a reason stronger than convenience:
 *  THERE IS NO NETWINDER OR CATS RIG ANYWHERE IN regress/ -- nothing in this tree boots a
 *  machine that has a footbridge.  So this is not the cheapest detector, it is the ONLY
 *  possible one.
 *
 *  IT IS THE REPOSITORY FILE.  This driver #includes the shipped dev_footbridge.c, exactly as
 *  diff_sh4_tmu.c #includes dev_sh4.c -- an identical header set, and the precedent that made
 *  the feasibility question answerable at all.  Nothing is transcribed.
 *
 *  SEVEN STUBS, and `-Wl,--gc-sections` is LOAD-BEARING rather than cosmetic: without it the
 *  link needs eleven more symbols (bus_pci_*, interrupt_*, console_start_slave,
 *  machine_add_tickfunction, memory_device_register).  The trade-off, stated so it is not
 *  mistaken for coverage: gc-sections drops DEVINIT, so this file cannot test devinit.
 *
 *  THE TWO STUBS THAT ARE INSTRUMENTS, not scaffolding: timer_add() and
 *  timer_update_frequency() RECORD the frequency the device asked for.  That is what lets a
 *  row assert the rate semantics directly, instead of inferring them from a boot that cannot
 *  happen here.
 *
 *  EXPECTED VALUES ARE ABSOLUTES DERIVED FROM THE 24-BIT WIDTH, never re-read from the header
 *  symbol the code itself uses.  R4's review established that a row which takes its expected
 *  value from the code under test is green for ANY value of it (filed as `constblind`), so
 *  16777216 is written out here on purpose.
 */
#include <inttypes.h>
#include <stdarg.h>
#include <stdbool.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <sys/wait.h>
#include <unistd.h>

#include "include/misc.h"
#include "include/memory.h"
#include "include/machine.h"
#include "include/cpu.h"

bool single_step = false;
bool about_to_enter_single_step = false;
static int verb[16];
int *debugmsg_current_verbosity = verb;

static int fatal_calls;
void fatal(const char *fmt, ...) { (void) fmt; fatal_calls++; }
void debug(const char *fmt, ...) { (void) fmt; }

/*  The recorder stubs: what rate did the device ASK the timer core for?  */
static double last_freq = -1.0;
static int timer_add_calls, timer_update_calls;
struct timer;
struct timer *timer_add(double freq, void (*f)(struct timer *, void *), void *extra)
{
	(void) f; (void) extra;
	last_freq = freq;
	timer_add_calls++;
	return (struct timer *) (void *) &last_freq;	/*  non-NULL cookie  */
}
void timer_update_frequency(struct timer *t, double freq)
{
	(void) t;
	last_freq = freq;
	timer_update_calls++;
}

uint64_t memory_readmax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len)
{
	uint64_t x = 0; int i;
	(void) cpu;
	/*  Little-endian, which is what the rigs are; the device only ever reads back what
	    this driver wrote, so the convention only has to be self-consistent.  */
	for (i = 0; i < len; i++)
		x |= (uint64_t) buf[i] << (8*i);
	return x;
}
void memory_writemax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len, uint64_t data)
{
	int i;
	(void) cpu;
	for (i = 0; i < len; i++)
		buf[i] = (data >> (8*i)) & 255;
}
void console_putchar(int handle, int ch) { (void) handle; (void) ch; }

/*  The device asserts an interrupt on every delivered tick, and INTERRUPT_ASSERT calls
    through a function pointer in the struct.  A calloc'd struct leaves that NULL, so the
    first tick SIGSEGVs -- which looks exactly like the crash under test and would have made
    this whole file a liar.  Install counting no-ops instead.  */
static int irq_asserts, irq_deasserts;
static void noop_assert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; irq_asserts++; }
static void noop_deassert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; irq_deasserts++; }

#include "../src/devices/dev_footbridge.c"

/*  Needs the struct, so it lives AFTER the device include.  */
static void arm_irqs(struct footbridge_data *d)
{
	int i;
	for (i = 0; i < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
		d->timer_irq[i].interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
		d->timer_irq[i].interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
	}
}

/*  #545 repair 3, X14: every other block's instance is a calloc with its interrupt fields seeded, counting calls
    on the CPU line d->irq and the four timer interrupts on ONE pair (noop_assert/noop_deassert).  X14 builds its
    instances here instead: the CPU line on its OWN pair, optionally DEVINIT's timer reset values, and a device
    line raised through the real footbridge_interrupt_assert() -- with footbridge_interrupt_deassert(), the only
    writers of irq_status and of irq_asserted.  (The Opus seat's scratch closure experiment, q2c pass; adapted.)  */
static int cpu_asserts, cpu_deasserts;
static void cpu_assert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; cpu_asserts++; }
static void cpu_deassert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; cpu_deasserts++; }
static struct footbridge_data *x14_new(uint32_t is, uint32_t ie, uint32_t fs, uint32_t fe, int devinit)
{
	struct footbridge_data *x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x));
	int i;
	arm_irqs(x);
	x->irq.interrupt_assert = cpu_assert;
	x->irq.interrupt_deassert = cpu_deassert;
	x->irq_status = is; x->irq_enable = ie; x->fiq_status = fs; x->fiq_enable = fe;
	if (devinit)
		for (i = 0; i < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
			x->timer_control[i] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC;
			x->timer_load[i] = TIMER_MAX_VAL;
			x->pending_bound[i] = 1;
		}
	return x;
}
static void x14_line(struct footbridge_data *x, int n)
{
	struct interrupt it;
	memset(&it, 0, sizeof(it));
	it.line = (uint32_t) 1 << n;
	it.extra = x;
	footbridge_interrupt_assert(&it);
}

/* --------------------------------------------------------------- harness --- */

static int rows, fails;
static void check(const char *name, const char *got, const char *want)
{
	rows++;
	if (strcmp(got, want) == 0)
		printf("  ok    %-54s %s\n", name, got);
	else {
		fails++;
		printf("  FAIL  %-54s\n          got  %s\n          want %s\n",
		    name, got, want);
	}
}
static void check_u(const char *name, uint64_t got, uint64_t want)
{
	char g[32], w[32];
	snprintf(g, sizeof(g), "%" PRIu64, got);
	snprintf(w, sizeof(w), "%" PRIu64, want);
	check(name, g, w);
}

/*  The helper is static inside the shipped file, which is exactly why this driver includes
    the file rather than linking it.  */
int main(void)
{
	struct footbridge_data *d;
	struct cpu *cpu;
	struct machine *machine;

	/*  LINE-BUFFERED ON PURPOSE.  gate_offline.sh redirects this binary's stdout to a file,
	    which makes it FULLY buffered -- so when a mutant re-introduces the crash, the log is
	    0 BYTES and the operator gets a red row with nothing in it.  Measured: line-buffered,
	    the same run leaves 943 bytes naming A1/B2/D1 as FAIL before it dies.  The gate's
	    greps fail either way, so this is legibility rather than detection -- but it is this
	    project's own recorded stdout-buffering trap, in a new place.  */
	setvbuf(stdout, NULL, _IOLBF, 0);

	cpu = calloc(1, sizeof(struct cpu));
	machine = calloc(1, sizeof(struct machine));
	d = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
	cpu->machine = machine;
	machine->emulated_hz = 100000000;	/*  100 MHz, an ordinary rate  */

	{
		/*
		 *  SELFCHECK -- CAN THIS FILE'S COMPARATOR STILL FAIL?
		 *
		 *  Feed check() deliberate mismatches and require the failure counter to move,
		 *  then un-record them so the rows are free.  If check() has been stubbed,
		 *  neutered, or had its comparison edited away, EVERY other row in this file is
		 *  silently inert and the gate cannot tell -- measured: 118 rows across five
		 *  detectors stayed green under exactly that stub, row counts, identity rows and
		 *  verdict tokens all intact.
		 *
		 *  TWO MISMATCHES, ONE ALPHABETIC AND ONE DIGIT-LEADING, and the second is not
		 *  padding.  The first version fed only "a" vs "b", and a pass-2 seat defeated it
		 *  with one line -- `isdigit(got[0]) || strcmp(...) == 0` -- because EVERY REAL ROW
		 *  IN THIS FILE COMPARES NUMBERS.  118 rows went inert, this sentinel stayed blind,
		 *  and four of the five gate-2 self-mutants stayed green too.  A sentinel whose
		 *  input does not resemble the rows it vouches for is a sentinel with a whitelist.
		 *
		 *  THE HEALTHY PATH PRINTS AN ok-SHAPED LINE, DELIBERATELY.  The first version
		 *  printed nothing on success and a FAIL line only on failure, which broke two
		 *  things at once: there was no stable string for gate 2 to grep, so DELETING THIS
		 *  WHOLE BLOCK FROM ALL FIVE FILES left the gate at PASS with the same 220 checks
		 *  and zero red rows -- and the FAIL text it printed on the HEALTHY path polluted
		 *  the gate's kill attribution, so a self-mutant pinned to a row id like "fail"
		 *  matched this line and reported OK for a mutant nothing had caught.
		 *
		 *  `>` not `== +1`: a comparator that double-counts compares perfectly well, and
		 *  the exact form printed "no longer compares" at it.  Direction was safe, the
		 *  diagnosis was wrong.
		 *
		 *  This does NOT prove any row is correct.  It proves the comparator still fails on
		 *  inputs shaped like the ones the rows use.  A comparator that compares
		 *  SELECTIVELY -- or that special-cases this row by name -- is still invisible to
		 *  it, and only a census would see that.  Liveness sentinel, nothing more.
		 *
		 *  THE PROBE ROWS ARE NAMED @@SELFCHECK@@ SO THEY CANNOT BE MISTAKEN FOR A ROW.
		 *  They are deliberate mismatches, so check() prints FAIL for them on the HEALTHY
		 *  path -- and a pass-2 seat measured that a self-mutant pinned to a row id like
		 *  "fail" matched one of those lines and reported OK for a mutant nothing caught.
		 *  The obvious cure is worse than the disease: suppressing the print INSIDE check()
		 *  would need a name test in the comparator, which is precisely the whitelist the
		 *  same seat showed defeats this sentinel (`strncmp(name,"SELFCHECK",9)`).  So the
		 *  comparator is left alone and the probe rows carry a token no real row would use;
		 *  selfmutant.py filters it, and gate 2 greps the SUMMARY line below for presence.
		 */
		int selfcheck_f = fails, selfcheck_r = rows, selfcheck_bad = 0;

		check("@@SELFCHECK@@/str", "a", "b");
		if (fails <= selfcheck_f)
			selfcheck_bad++;
		fails = selfcheck_f; rows = selfcheck_r;

		check("@@SELFCHECK@@/num", "1", "2");
		if (fails <= selfcheck_f)
			selfcheck_bad++;
		fails = selfcheck_f; rows = selfcheck_r;

		if (selfcheck_bad) {
			printf("  FAIL  SELFCHECK the comparator no longer compares (%d of 2 "
			    "mismatches went unnoticed) -- every row in this file is inert\n",
			    selfcheck_bad);
			fails = selfcheck_f + 1;
			rows = selfcheck_r;
		} else {
			printf("  ok    SELFCHECK the comparator can still fail       "
			    "str+num\n");
		}
	}

	printf("--- A. the load interpretation: zero is a FULL PERIOD, never a divisor of 0 ---\n");
	/*  16777216 == 2^24, written as an absolute rather than read from TIMER_MAX_VAL: a row
	    that takes its expected value from the code under test cannot notice the code
	    changing (the `constblind` finding).  */
	check_u("A1 load 0 becomes the full 24-bit span",
	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0, 0, 0), 16777216);
	check_u("A2 an ordinary load is untouched",
	    footbridge_effective_cycles(1000, 0, 0), 1000);
	check_u("A3 the maximum load is untouched",
	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0xffffff, 0, 0), 16777215);

	printf("--- B. the prescaler is 64-bit MULTIPLICATION, not a signed shift ---\n");
	/*  THE ROW THAT CATCHES THE DEFECT THE OBVIOUS FIX RE-CREATES.  The old code used
	    `int cycles` with `cycles <<= 8`; three seats found independently that this
	    overflows for exactly half the legal range, and that mapping zero to 2^24 while
	    leaving `int` gives 2^24 << 8 == 2^32 == 0 -> +INF again.  B1 and B2 are the two
	    halves of that: the reset load prescaled, and the zero load prescaled.  */
	check_u("B1 reset load x256 does not overflow",
	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0xffffff, TIMER_FCLK_256, 1),
	    (uint64_t) 16777215 * 256);
	check_u("B2 zero load x256 does not wrap to zero",
	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0, TIMER_FCLK_256, 1),
	    (uint64_t) 16777216 * 256);
	check_u("B3 the x16 prescaler", footbridge_effective_cycles(1000, TIMER_FCLK_16, 1),
	    16000);

	printf("--- C. TIMER_EXTERNAL is a LEGAL encoding and must not kill the host ---\n");
	/*  Both prescaler bits set is TIMER_EXTERNAL (0x0C).  The order of the tests matters:
	    with a bare `else if` chain, 0x0C reads as FCLK_16 by accident.  */
	check_u("C1 TIMER_EXTERNAL is not silently read as x16",
	    footbridge_effective_cycles(1000, TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256, 1), 1000);

	printf("--- D. THE TWO CONSUMERS LIVE IN DIFFERENT DOMAINS ---\n");
	/*  THE ROW A TENTH READING FORCED.  timer_value is a 24-bit GUEST-READABLE register.
	    Feeding it the PRESCALED span would store values far above 24 bits -- the measuring
	    seat's own first proposal did exactly that and put 19833 of 20000 ticks out of
	    range.  The prescaler slows the RATE; it does not widen the COUNTER.  */
	check_u("D1 the counter domain ignores the prescaler",
	    footbridge_effective_cycles(0, TIMER_FCLK_256, 0), 16777216);
	{
		int i, over = 0;
		arm_irqs(d);
		d->timer_load[0] = 0;
		d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256;
		d->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 1;
		for (i = 0; i < 20000; i++) {
			dev_footbridge_tick(cpu, d);
			if (d->timer_value[0] > 0xffffff)
				over++;
		}
		check_u("D2 20000 ticks leave the 24-bit counter in range", over, 0);
	}
	/*  D3 EXISTS BECAUSE D2 WAS NOT ENOUGH, and the review measured why: every tick-path
	    row above uses load == 0, and for load == 0 the constant 16777216 is exactly the
	    right answer -- so replacing the whole helper call with that literal passed the
	    entire file.  The counter must TRACK THE GUEST'S LOAD, not merely stay in range.  */
	{
		int i, over = 0;
		struct footbridge_data *e = calloc(1, sizeof(*e));
		arm_irqs(e);
		e->timer_load[0] = 1000;
		e->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
		e->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = 1;
		for (i = 0; i < 20000; i++) {
			dev_footbridge_tick(cpu, e);
			if (e->timer_value[0] >= 1000)
				over++;
		}
		check_u("D3 the counter stays below the guest's OWN load", over, 0);
		free(e);
	}

	printf("--- E. the rate the device asks the timer core for ---\n");
	memset(d, 0, sizeof(*d));
	d->timer_load[0] = 1000;
	d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
	last_freq = -1.0;
	reload_timer_value(cpu, d, 0);
	check_u("E1 a 1000-cycle load at 100 MHz asks for 100 kHz",
	    (uint64_t) (last_freq + 0.5), 100000);
	/*  E2 is the route that needs NO load write at all: the reset load is TIMER_MAX_VAL,
	    so one write of ENABLE|FCLK_256 reaches the old overflow.  Before #432 this asked
	    for a NEGATIVE rate, which the core's floor clamp turned into one tick per ~3.2
	    years, silently.  */
	d->timer_load[0] = 0xffffff;
	d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256;
	last_freq = -1.0;
	reload_timer_value(cpu, d, 0);
	check(  "E2 the reset load x256 asks for a POSITIVE rate",
	    last_freq > 0.0 ? "positive" : "NEGATIVE OR ZERO", "positive");
	/*  E3: a zero load must ask for a slow, SERVICEABLE rate -- not the domain ceiling.
	    100e6 / 2^24 is about 5.96 Hz.  */
	d->timer_load[0] = 0;
	d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
	last_freq = -1.0;
	reload_timer_value(cpu, d, 0);
	check(  "E3 a zero load asks for a few Hz, not the ceiling",
	    (last_freq > 1.0 && last_freq < 100.0) ? "serviceable" : "OUT OF RANGE",
	    "serviceable");
	/*  E4 EXISTS BECAUSE E2 WAS A SIGN-ONLY ORACLE, and the review measured the cost of
	    that: changing the `1` in reload_timer_value()'s helper call to a `0` -- ONE
	    CHARACTER -- silently deletes the prescaler from the RATE domain for every guest,
	    and all fifteen rows stayed green.  Sections B and C prove the HELPER prescales;
	    D1/D2 prove the COUNTER consumer does not; nothing pinned the RATE consumer to
	    prescaling, which is the round's headline design decision.  100 MHz / (1000 * 256)
	    is 390.625 Hz; the mutant asks for 100000, a rate 256x too fast.  */
	d->timer_load[0] = 1000;
	d->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_256;
	last_freq = -1.0;
	reload_timer_value(cpu, d, 0);
	check_u("E4 the RATE consumer really applies the x256 prescaler",
	    (uint64_t) (last_freq * 1000.0 + 0.5), 390625);

	printf("--- F. END TO END: the shipped tick survives route 2, in a forked child ---\n");
	/*  ROUTE 2, the one that PREDATES #427/#428 and is the reason this round does not get
	    to blame its own hardening: TIMER_x_LOAD does not clear pending_timer_interrupts
	    (TIMER_x_CONTROL does), so an ordinary legal timer accrues a backlog and a later
	    write of zero divides.  Forked, because a mutant that re-crashes would otherwise
	    take the whole gate binary down and score as a census FAULT rather than a named-row
	    KILL.  */
	{
		pid_t pid = fork();
		if (pid == 0) {
			struct footbridge_data *c = calloc(1, sizeof(*c));
			int i;
			arm_irqs(c);
			c->timer_load[0] = 50000;	/*  an ordinary legal timer  */
			c->timer_control[0] = TIMER_ENABLE;
			for (i = 0; i < 400; i++)
				c->pending_timer_interrupts[0]++;
			c->timer_load[0] = 0;		/*  the guest writes zero  */
			dev_footbridge_tick(cpu, c);
			_exit(0);
		} else {
			int st = 0;
			waitpid(pid, &st, 0);
			check("F1 a zero load after a backlog does not kill the host",
			    WIFEXITED(st) && WEXITSTATUS(st) == 0 ? "survived"
			    : "KILLED BY SIGNAL", "survived");
		}
	}

	printf("--- G. the CONTROL write path: a legal encoding must not exit() ---\n");
	/*  THE ROW THAT DEFENDS THE FOLD.  Section C proves the helper INTERPRETS 0x0C; this
	    proves the guest can actually WRITE it.  Before #432 this exact write reached
	    fatal()+exit(1) in the CONTROL arm -- a guest selecting the external clock source
	    killed the host, and no amount of helper correctness would have saved it.  Forked,
	    because a mutant that restores the exit(1) would otherwise take the gate binary
	    down and score as a census FAULT instead of a named-row KILL.  */
	{
		pid_t pid = fork();
		if (pid == 0) {
			struct footbridge_data *c = calloc(1, sizeof(*c));
			unsigned char buf[4];
			arm_irqs(c);
			c->timer_load[0] = 1000;
			memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
			buf[0] = TIMER_ENABLE | TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256;
			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_CONTROL, buf,
			    4, MEM_WRITE, c);
			_exit(0);
		} else {
			int st = 0;
			waitpid(pid, &st, 0);
			check("G1 a guest write of ENABLE|EXTERNAL does not exit()",
			    WIFEXITED(st) && WEXITSTATUS(st) == 0 ? "survived"
			    : "HOST KILLED", "survived");
		}
	}

	printf("--- H. the LOAD register reads back EXACTLY what the guest wrote ---\n");
	/*  THE FIFTH-VACUITY-CLASS ROW, and the adjudicating seat found it by attacking its own
	    design: "timer_load[] is never rewritten -- the guest reads back exactly what it
	    wrote" is a shipped, load-bearing claim, and it had NO DETECTOR.  Reinstating
	    normalize-at-write -- `timer_load = idata ? idata : 1`, precisely the alternative
	    this round REJECTED -- passed all fifteen rows, because nothing in this file ever
	    drove TIMER_1_LOAD through the access handler; every other row pokes struct fields
	    directly.  These two rows go through dev_footbridge_access in both directions.  */
	{
		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(*h));
		unsigned char buf[4];
		uint64_t back;
		arm_irqs(h);

		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
		check_u("H1 a guest write of 0 reads back as 0, not normalised", back, 0);

		/*  H2 pins the 24-bit MASK as well: 0x1000000 is "one full wrap", and the mask
		    aliases it to zero -- which is the arithmetic that makes the full-period
		    mapping the continuous choice in the first place.  */
		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
		memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x1000000);
		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
		memset(buf, 0xff, sizeof(buf));
		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, TIMER_1_LOAD, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
		back = memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4);
		check_u("H2 0x1000000 is masked to 0, and stays 0 on read-back", back, 0);
		free(h);
	}

	/*  fbclearread: `case TIMER_1_CLEAR:` carries NO writeflag test, while the case
	    immediately above it (TIMER_1_CONTROL) does.  So a guest READ runs the whole
	    body: it reloads the counter when periodic, decrements the pending count, and
	    DEASSERTS the interrupt.  A guest that merely reads the register silently
	    acknowledges a pending timer interrupt -- the likely guest-visible consequence
	    is a LOST interrupt, not a corrupted value.

	    FOUR addresses, not one: the timer fold normalises 0x30C/0x32C/0x34C/0x36C onto
	    this single case.

	    These rows drive the ACCESS HANDLER rather than poking struct fields, for the
	    reason this file already records above: a struct-poking row cannot see a
	    handler-level defect at all.  */
	{
		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(*h));
		unsigned char buf[4];
		uint64_t back;

		arm_irqs(h);


 succeeded in 474ms:

1236: #define YNEW(s) do { x = calloc(1, sizeof(*x)); arm_irqs(x); x->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert; \
1237: 		x->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert; x->irq_status = SY[s].is; x->irq_enable = SY[s].ie; \
1238: 		x->fiq_status = SY[s].fs; x->fiq_enable = SY[s].fe; } while (0)
1239: #define YSAME(s) (x->irq_status == SY[s].is && x->irq_enable == SY[s].ie && x->fiq_status == SY[s].fs \
1240: 		&& x->fiq_enable == SY[s].fe)
1241: #define YDECLINE_ALL() do { for (si = 0; si < 5; si++) { memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70); \
1242: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, DY[si].addr, b, 4, DY[si].wf, x); } } while (0)
1243: 		/*  X10  */
1244: 		for (st = 0; st < 4; st++) {
1245: 			for (r = 0; r < 5; r++)
1246: 				for (w = 0; w < (r == 0 || r == 2 ? 4 : 3); w++) {
1247: 					uint32_t is = SY[st].is, ie = SY[st].ie, fs = SY[st].fs, fe = SY[st].fe;
1248: 					uint64_t want = r == 0 ? (uint64_t) (is & ie) : r == 1 ? (uint64_t) is
1249: 					    : r == 2 ? (uint64_t) (fs & fe) : r == 3 ? (uint64_t) fs : (uint64_t) ie;
1250: 					want &= WY[w] == 8 ? ~0ULL : WY[w] == 4 ? 0xffffffffULL : WY[w] == 2 ? 0xffffULL : 0xffULL;
1251: 					YNEW(st);
1252: 					YDECLINE_ALL();
1253: 					f0 = fatal_calls; a0 = irq_asserts; d0 = irq_deasserts;
1254: 					memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b));
1255: 					rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, RY[r].addr, b, WY[w], MEM_READ, x);
1256: 					ok = rv == 1 && fatal_calls == f0 && memory_readmax64(cpu, b, WY[w]) == want && YSAME(st)
1257: 					    && irq_asserts == a0 && irq_deasserts == d0;
1258: 					snprintf(yn, sizeof(yn), "X10 legal len %d read of %s (%s, latches SET) returns its value",
1259: 					    WY[w], RY[r].nm, SY[st].nm);
1260: 					check_u(yn, ok, 1);
1261: 					free(x);
1262: 				}
1263: 			{
1264: 				uint32_t is = SY[st].is, ne = SY[st].ie & ~0x70u;
1265: 				YNEW(st);
1266: 				YDECLINE_ALL();
1267: 				f0 = fatal_calls; a0 = irq_asserts; d0 = irq_deasserts;
1268: 				memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
1269: 				rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
1270: 				ok = rv == 1 && fatal_calls == f0 && x->irq_enable == ne && x->irq_status == is
1271: 				    && x->fiq_status == SY[st].fs && x->fiq_enable == SY[st].fe
1272: 				    && ((is & ne) ? (irq_asserts == a0 + 1 && irq_deasserts == d0)
1273: 				                  : (irq_asserts == a0 && irq_deasserts == d0 + 1));
1274: 				snprintf(yn, sizeof(yn), "X10 IRQ Clear write 0x70 (%s, latches SET) clears and drives", SY[st].nm);
1275: 				check_u(yn, ok, 1);
1276: 				free(x);
1277: 			}
1278: 		}
1279: 		/*  X11  */
1280: 		for (st = 0; st < 4; st++)
1281: 			for (j = 0; j < 5; j++)
1282: 				for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
1283: 					if (i == j)
1284: 						continue;
1285: 					YNEW(st);
1286: 					memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
1287: 					dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, DY[j].addr, b, 4, DY[j].wf, x);
1288: 					f0 = fatal_calls; a0 = irq_asserts; d0 = irq_deasserts;
1289: 					memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
1290: 					if (DY[i].wf == MEM_READ)
1291: 						memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b));
1292: 					rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, DY[i].addr, b, 4, DY[i].wf, x);
1293: 					ok = rv == 1 && fatal_calls - f0 == 1 && YSAME(st) && irq_asserts == a0 && irq_deasserts == d0
1294: 					    && (DY[i].wf != MEM_READ || memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4) == 0);
1295: 					f0 = fatal_calls;
1296: 					memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
1297: 					dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, DY[j].addr, b, 4, DY[j].wf, x);
1298: 					memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70);
1299: 					dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, DY[i].addr, b, 4, DY[i].wf, x);
1300: 					ok = ok && fatal_calls == f0 && YSAME(st) && irq_asserts == a0 && irq_deasserts == d0;
1301: 					if (st == 0)
1302: 						snprintf(yn, sizeof(yn), "X11 first %s after %s latched: announced once", DY[i].nm, DY[j].nm);
1303: 					else
1304: 						snprintf(yn, sizeof(yn), "X11 first %s after %s latched (%s): announced once",
1305: 						    DY[i].nm, DY[j].nm, SY[st].nm);
1306: 					check_u(yn, ok, 1);
1307: 					free(x);
1308: 				}
1309: 		/*  X12  */
1310: 		for (st = 0; st < 4; st++)
1311: 			for (c = 0; c < 5; c++) {
1312: 				uint64_t mask = CY[c].w == 4 ? 0xffffffffULL : CY[c].w == 2 ? 0xffffULL : 0xffULL;
1313: 				uint32_t is = SY[st].is, ne = SY[st].ie & ~(uint32_t) (CY[c].data & mask);
1314: 				YNEW(st);
1315: 				fatal_calls = 0; a0 = irq_asserts; d0 = irq_deasserts;
1316: 				memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b));      /*  3d: the bytes above the width are NOT zero  */
1317: 				memory_writemax64(cpu, b, CY[c].w, CY[c].data);
1318: 				rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, CY[c].w, MEM_WRITE, x);
1319: 				ok = rv == 1 && fatal_calls == 0 && x->irq_enable == ne && x->irq_status == is
1320: 				    && x->fiq_status == SY[st].fs && x->fiq_enable == SY[st].fe
1321: 				    && ((is & ne) ? (irq_asserts == a0 + 1 && irq_deasserts == d0)
1322: 				                  : (irq_asserts == a0 && irq_deasserts == d0 + 1));
1323: 				YDECLINE_ALL();
1324: 				ok = ok && fatal_calls == 5;
1325: 				snprintf(yn, sizeof(yn), "X12 IRQ Clear write %s (%s, latches clear)", CY[c].nm, SY[st].nm);
1326: 				check_u(yn, ok, 1);
1327: 				free(x);
1328: 			}
1329: 		/*  X13  */
1330: 		for (st = 0; st < 4; st++)
1331: 			for (c = 0; c < 5; c++) {
1332: 				uint64_t mask = CY[c].w == 4 ? 0xffffffffULL : CY[c].w == 2 ? 0xffffULL : 0xffULL;
1333: 				uint32_t is = SY[st].is, ne = SY[st].ie & ~(uint32_t) (CY[c].data & mask);
1334: 				YNEW(st);
1335: 				YDECLINE_ALL();
1336: 				f0 = fatal_calls; a0 = irq_asserts; d0 = irq_deasserts;
1337: 				memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b));      /*  3d: the bytes above the width are NOT zero  */
1338: 				memory_writemax64(cpu, b, CY[c].w, CY[c].data);
1339: 				rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, CY[c].w, MEM_WRITE, x);
1340: 				ok = rv == 1 && fatal_calls == f0 && x->irq_enable == ne && x->irq_status == is
1341: 				    && x->fiq_status == SY[st].fs && x->fiq_enable == SY[st].fe
1342: 				    && ((is & ne) ? (irq_asserts == a0 + 1 && irq_deasserts == d0)
1343: 				                  : (irq_asserts == a0 && irq_deasserts == d0 + 1));
1344: 				YDECLINE_ALL();
1345: 				ok = ok && fatal_calls == f0;
1346: 				snprintf(yn, sizeof(yn), "X13 IRQ Clear write %s (%s, latches SET)", CY[c].nm, SY[st].nm);
1347: 				check_u(yn, ok, 1);
1348: 				free(x);
1349: 			}
1350: #undef YDECLINE_ALL
1351: #undef YSAME
1352: #undef YNEW
1353: 		fatal_calls = 0;
1354: 	}
1355: 	/*
1356: 	 *  X14 (repair 3): the cells no block above can reach, because of HOW ITS INSTANCES ARE BUILT.  The Opus seat
1357: 	 *  measured 28 one-line edits green on 707 rows (q2_20260923/q2c_opus/SUMMARY.txt), the smallest ONE
1358: 	 *  character -- `&` -> `&&` in the Clear write's drive test.  Every instance above is a calloc with its
1359: 	 *  interrupt fields seeded, so:
1360: 	 *    no row CHECKED the drive test (irq_status & the new enable) with both operands nonzero and DISJOINT --
1361: 	 *    P8 makes exactly that access and checks only the enable (codex, repair 3's pass) -- nor with the status
1362: 	 *    zero and the enable not, nor with one of the other lines a device raises pending alone (timers 5-7, ISA
1363: 	 *    10 on cats, 11 on netwinder, PCI 18 on cats): where it was checked it was zero with an operand zero, or
1364: 	 *    nonzero with bit 4 or bit 16 in it;
1365: 	 *    the CPU line and the four timer interrupts share one counter pair, so no row could see a Clear write
1366: 	 *    drive the WRONG interrupt;
1367: 	 *    in the #545 blocks the timer fields sat at 0 where DEVINIT sets PERIODIC, TIMER_MAX_VAL and a bound of 1,
1368: 	 *    and no tick was ever pending (the timer rows at the top of this file seed ticks; they test other arms);
1369: 	 *    irq_asserted was never 1: only the producer pair writes it (assert sets it, deassert clears it), and no
1370: 	 *    block called either.
1371: 	 *  Every row here that checks the interrupt output also requires the shared pair UNMOVED: the CPU line was
1372: 	 *  driven, or left alone, and no timer interrupt was touched.  Repair 3c (this block's own pass -- the Opus seat
1373: 	 *  measured nine edits at cells this header claimed, on channels its rows did not read: a Clear's drive at
1374: 	 *  DEVINIT values in either polarity, a decline driving at those values (TD04) or while irq_asserted (IA14),
1375: 	 *  the status read clearing or driving while irq_asserted, the reads after the latches set returning 0) gave
1376: 	 *  the DEVINIT reads, Set/Clear and declines, the IRQ_STATUS read and
1377: 	 *  the re-arm row the drive, the fields and the read values they claimed, and added the DEVINIT Clear that
1378: 	 *  keeps a line pending (q2_20260923/q2e_opus/SUMMARY.txt).  Repair 3d (3c's own pass): five of those checks
1379: 	 *  now read what their names and COVERED claim -- the raw status at the DEVINIT Clear 0x20 (codex, grok, glm:
1380: 	 *  a Clear zeroing it there passed) and at the asserted Clears (the Opus seat's FS01), the FIQ fields at the
1381: 	 *  IRQ_STATUS read and the re-arm row (grok: a read setting fiq_enable passed), and irq_asserted at the
1382: 	 *  re-arm row (a decline clearing it passed) -- with no new row (q2_20260923/r3d_20260923).
1383: 	 *  Width 8 is X6/X7/X9/X10's; +0x184, +0x18c and +0x284 cannot take an 8-byte access.
1384: 	 */
1385: 	{
1386: 		static const int LINE[7] = { 4, 5, 6, 7, 10, 11, 18 };
1387: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; uint32_t want; } RR[5] = {
1388: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, 0x10 }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, 0x18 }, { FIQ_STATUS, 0x40 }, { FIQ_RAW_STATUS, 0x60 },
1389: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_SET, 0x30 } };
1390: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; } DD[5] = {
1391: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE }, { IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ },
1392: 			{ FIQ_STATUS, MEM_WRITE }, { FIQ_RAW_STATUS, MEM_WRITE } };
1393: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; uint32_t want; } RA[5] = {
1394: 			{ IRQ_STATUS, 0x10 }, { IRQ_RAW_STATUS, 0x10 }, { FIQ_STATUS, 0 }, { FIQ_RAW_STATUS, 0 },
1395: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_SET, 0x10 } };
1396: 		static const char *TS[2] = { "DEVINIT timers", "DEVINIT timers, a tick pending" };
1397: 		unsigned char b[8];
1398: 		char zn[200];
1399: 		int i, k, tk, a0, d0, f0, rv, ok;
1400: 		struct footbridge_data *x;
1401: #define ZZERO() do { cpu_asserts = cpu_deasserts = 0; a0 = irq_asserts; d0 = irq_deasserts; fatal_calls = 0; } while (0)
1402: #define ZCPU(as, ds) (cpu_asserts == (as) && cpu_deasserts == (ds) && irq_asserts == a0 && irq_deasserts == d0)
1403: #define ZCLR(v) do { memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, (v)); \
1404: 		rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x); } while (0)
1405: #define ZDECLINE() do { for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) { memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b)); \
1406: 		if (DD[i].wf == MEM_WRITE) memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x70); \
1407: 		rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, DD[i].addr, b, 4, DD[i].wf, x); \
1408: 		ok = ok && rv == 1 && (DD[i].wf == MEM_WRITE || memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4) == 0); } } while (0)
1409: 		/*  The drive test's operand cells: both nonzero and disjoint (`&&` for `&` asserts there), and the status
1410: 		    zero with the enable not (a test of the enable alone asserts there).  */
1411: 		x = x14_new(0x18, 0x30, 0x60, 0xc0, 0);
1412: 		ZZERO();
1413: 		ZCLR(0x10);
1414: 		check_u("X14 Clear 0x10 from 0x18/0x30 (disjoint): 0x20, CPU line deasserted once",
1415: 		    rv == 1 && x->irq_enable == 0x20 && ZCPU(0, 1) && fatal_calls == 0, 1);
1416: 		free(x);
1417: 		x = x14_new(0, 0x30, 0, 0, 0);
1418: 		ZZERO();
1419: 		ZCLR(0x10);
1420: 		check_u("X14 Clear 0x10 from 0/0x30 (no status): 0x20, CPU line deasserted once",
1421: 		    rv == 1 && x->irq_enable == 0x20 && ZCPU(0, 1) && fatal_calls == 0, 1);
1422: 		free(x);
1423: 		/*  Each line a device raises, pending and enabled ALONE: a Clear of nothing asserts the CPU line.  */
1424: 		for (k = 0; k < 7; k++) {
1425: 			x = x14_new((uint32_t) 1 << LINE[k], (uint32_t) 1 << LINE[k], 0, 0, 0);
1426: 			ZZERO();
1427: 			ZCLR(0);
1428: 			snprintf(zn, sizeof(zn), "X14 Clear 0, only line %d pending and enabled: CPU line asserted once",
1429: 			    LINE[k]);
1430: 			check_u(zn, rv == 1 && x->irq_enable == (uint32_t) 1 << LINE[k] && ZCPU(1, 0) && fatal_calls == 0, 1);
1431: 			free(x);
1432: 		}
1433: 		/*  DEVINIT's timer reset values, then with a tick pending: the five legal reads return their values and
1434: 		    announce nothing; a Set then a Clear land; the five declines announce exactly 5 and keep the fields.  */
1435: 		for (tk = 0; tk < 2; tk++) {
1436: 			x = x14_new(0x18, 0x30, 0x60, 0xc0, 1);
1437: 			x->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = tk;
1438: 			ZZERO();
1439: 			ok = 1;
1440: 			for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
1441: 				memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b));
1442: 				rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, RR[i].addr, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
1443: 				ok = ok && rv == 1 && memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4) == RR[i].want;
1444: 			}
1445: 			snprintf(zn, sizeof(zn), "X14 %s: the five legal reads return their values", TS[tk]);
1446: 			check_u(zn, ok && fatal_calls == 0 && ZCPU(0, 0), 1);
1447: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, b, 4, 0x40);
1448: 			rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_SET, b, 4, MEM_WRITE, x);
1449: 			ok = rv == 1;
1450: 			ZCLR(0x10);
1451: 			snprintf(zn, sizeof(zn), "X14 %s: Set 0x40 then Clear 0x10 leave 0x60", TS[tk]);
1452: 			check_u(zn, ok && rv == 1 && x->irq_enable == 0x60 && fatal_calls == 0 && ZCPU(1, 1), 1);
1453: 			ZZERO();
1454: 			ok = 1;
1455: 			ZDECLINE();
1456: 			snprintf(zn, sizeof(zn), "X14 %s: the five declines announce 5, keep the fields", TS[tk]);
1457: 			check_u(zn, ok && fatal_calls == 5 && x->irq_status == 0x18 && x->irq_enable == 0x60
1458: 			    && x->fiq_status == 0x60 && x->fiq_enable == 0xc0 && ZCPU(0, 0), 1);
1459: 			free(x);
1460: 			/*  repair 3c: the other polarity of the drive at these values -- a Clear that leaves an enabled line
1461: 			    pending must ASSERT (the row above ends where the drive deasserts); 3d: and the raw status stays
1462: 			    0x18, as the name says (codex, grok, glm: a Clear that zeroed it here passed every row)  */
1463: 			x = x14_new(0x18, 0x30, 0x60, 0xc0, 1);
1464: 			x->pending_timer_interrupts[0] = tk;
1465: 			ZZERO();
1466: 			ZCLR(0x20);
1467: 			snprintf(zn, sizeof(zn), "X14 %s: Clear 0x20 keeps 0x10 pending and enabled: CPU line asserted once", TS[tk]);
1468: 			check_u(zn, rv == 1 && x->irq_enable == 0x10 && x->irq_status == 0x18 && ZCPU(1, 0) && fatal_calls == 0,
1469: 			    1);
1470: 			free(x);
1471: 		}
1472: 		/*  irq_asserted = 1, through the real callback: a device raises line 4 with line 4 enabled.  */
1473: 		x = x14_new(0, 0x10, 0, 0, 0);
1474: 		ZZERO();
1475: 		x14_line(x, 4);
1476: 		ok = x->irq_asserted == 1 && x->irq_status == 0x10 && ZCPU(1, 0);
1477: 		ZZERO();
1478: 		memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b));
1479: 		rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
1480: 		check_u("X14 line 4 raised by a device: IRQ_STATUS reads 0x10",
1481: 		    ok && rv == 1 && memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4) == 0x10 && fatal_calls == 0 && x->irq_status == 0x10
1482: 		    && x->irq_enable == 0x10 && x->irq_asserted == 1 && x->fiq_status == 0 && x->fiq_enable == 0
1483: 		    && ZCPU(0, 0), 1);
1484: 		free(x);
1485: 		for (k = 0; k < 2; k++) {
1486: 			x = x14_new(0, 0x10, 0, 0, 0);
1487: 			x14_line(x, 4);
1488: 			ok = x->irq_asserted == 1;
1489: 			ZZERO();
1490: 			ZCLR(k ? 0x400 : 0x10);
1491: 			snprintf(zn, sizeof(zn), "X14 line 4 raised by a device: Clear %s, the raw status kept, CPU line %s once",
1492: 			    k ? "0x400 keeps it" : "0x10 clears it", k ? "asserted" : "deasserted");
1493: 			/*  3d: a Clear writes the enable only -- the raw status is the sources' (the Opus seat's FS01, a Clear
1494: 			    dropping the masked line from it, passed).  irq_asserted is NOT pinned here: the enable arms never
1495: 			    write it, which is `fbirqstuck`'s defect, and a row pinning it would pin that defect.  */
1496: 			check_u(zn, ok && rv == 1 && x->irq_enable == (k ? 0x10u : 0u) && x->irq_status == 0x10
1497: 			    && (k ? ZCPU(1, 0) : ZCPU(0, 1)) && fatal_calls == 0, 1);
1498: 			free(x);
1499: 		}
1500: 		x = x14_new(0, 0x10, 0, 0, 0);
1501: 		x14_line(x, 4);
1502: 		ok = x->irq_asserted == 1;
1503: 		ZZERO();
1504: 		ZDECLINE();
1505: 		f0 = fatal_calls;
1506: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
1507: 			memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof(b));
1508: 			rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, RA[i].addr, b, 4, MEM_READ, x);
1509: 			ok = ok && rv == 1 && memory_readmax64(cpu, b, 4) == RA[i].want;
1510: 		}
1511: 		ZCLR(0x400);
1512: 		ok = ok && rv == 1;
1513: 		ZDECLINE();
1514: 		check_u("X14 line 4 raised by a device: 5 declines, then the legal reads and a Clear re-arm no latch",
1515: 		    ok && f0 == 5 && fatal_calls == 5 && ZCPU(1, 0) && x->irq_status == 0x10 && x->irq_enable == 0x10
1516: 		    && x->irq_asserted == 1 && x->fiq_status == 0 && x->fiq_enable == 0, 1);
1517: 		free(x);
1518: #undef ZDECLINE
1519: #undef ZCLR
1520: #undef ZCPU
1521: #undef ZZERO
1522: 		fatal_calls = 0;
1523: 	}
1524: 	/*  IDENTITY, as every differential here carries.  #545: 33 -> 333 (the 33 are the 32 timer rows and this one;
1525: 	    then 78 P-rows + 222 X-rows); the adjudication pass, 2026-09-23: 333 -> 491 (X6 +54 and X7 +35 for the wide
1526: 	    state, X9 69); its repair's own pass 2: 491 -> 707 (the ones state: X6 +54, X7 +35, X9 +23; X10 64, X11 20,
1527: 	    X12 20); the closure's closing pass (repair 3): 707 -> 862 (the 8-byte cells: X6 +32, X7 +8, X9 +8, X10 +8;
1528: 	    X11's other three states +60; X13 20; X14 19); repair 3's own pass (3c): 862 -> 864 (X14 +2); 3c's own
1529: 	    pass (3d): 864, no row added -- five X14 checks read more, and X12/X13's buffer bytes above the width.  */
1530: 	{
1531: 		char buf[32];
1532: 		snprintf(buf, sizeof(buf), "%d", rows + 1);
1533: 		check("IDENTITY row count -- guards against a stale copy", buf, "864");
1534: 	}
1535: 
1536: 	printf("\n%d rows, %d failures\n", rows, fails);
1537: 	printf("DIFF_FOOTBRIDGE_%s\n", fails == 0 ? "PASS" : "FAIL");
1538: 	return fails ? 1 : 0;

822: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {

824: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, s5[i].addr, b, 4, s5[i].wf, x);
825: 		}
826: 		check_u("X3 legal ZERO-data enable writes and other-register reads re-arm NO latch", fatal_calls - f0, 0);
827: 		free(x);
828: 		/*  X4 (codex, pass 2): the FIRST access carries ZERO -- a zero-data write, or the +0x18c read with
829: 		    IRQEnable 0.  A latch guard conditioned on the data (`&& idata`), a +0x18c guard conditioned on
830: 		    irq_enable, or a direction test rewritten as `!idata`, lets such an access through without an
831: 		    announce; each must announce once and change no field.  (A WRITE-site guard conditioned on an
832: 		    enable -- X4 keeps the seeded enables there -- is X6's all-zero-state cells, not X4's.)  */

846: 		}

865: 	 *
866: 	 *  X6: EVERY DECLINED ACCESS -- site (5) x width (1, 2, 4, and 8 at the two 8-byte-aligned sites) x data (zero,
867: 	 *      nonzero; writes only) x state (seeded, all-zero, wide, ones) x occurrence (first, repeated).  Each row
868: 	 *      requires: the handler returns 1; the announce count moves by exactly 1 on the first access, 0 on the
869: 	 *      repeat; all four register fields are unchanged; the interrupt output is untouched (no assert, no
870: 	 *      deassert); and a declined read returns 0 over its whole width.

874: 	 *      states, so a divert keyed on a zero field or on any one bit announces in one of them.  After each, the

876: 	 *      of a legal access with the latches set is X10's.

878: 	 *  (value and effect), X11 the cross-site latch ORDER and PRESERVATION, X12 and X13 the legal Clear write's
879: 	 *  width and data with the latches clear and set.

883: 	 *  direction test; the WIDTH, whose domain is what the ARM load/store unit emits -- 1, 2 and 4, and 8 at the
884: 	 *  8-byte-aligned offsets: of the five decline sites, +0x180 and +0x280 (an LDRD/STRD there is ONE 8-byte

890: 	 *  fields (read by the legal branches); and the five latches (each read by its own decline).  The bodies
891: 	 *  compare no field or data against a constant: they test the direction, their own latch and, in the Clear

894: 	 *  _deassert() are the only writers of irq_status, and the lines a device raises are the timers (4-7), ISA 10
895: 	 *  (cats) and 11 (netwinder) and PCI 18 (cats); no device raises the other 25.  And an edit can condition any

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""#545 r3d table, variant generation (WSL).  /tmp/fbd5r3d_var gets:
  the r3c table's 711 variant files and its FIX.c, copied from /tmp/fbd5r3c_var (FIX.c must be SEC's device);
  OQ_<id>.c  -- the Opus seat's 22 q2f shapes (its gen_q2f.py, applied to the same device);
  R3D_<id>.c -- the new shapes of 3c's pass that r3d's checks are for, plus two controls.
Every anchor must occur exactly once in the device; a shape whose text does not change the file is refused."""
import hashlib, io, os, shutil, sys

R = "/mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL"
SEC = R + "/GXEMUL-SEC"
OLD = "/tmp/fbd5r3c_var"
V = "/tmp/fbd5r3d_var"
DEV = SEC + "/src/devices/dev_footbridge.c"
T, NL = "\t", "\n"


def md5(p):
    return hashlib.md5(open(p, "rb").read()).hexdigest()


base = io.open(DEV, encoding="utf-8", newline="").read()
assert md5(DEV).startswith("783c0c7f"), "SEC device md5"
shutil.rmtree(V, ignore_errors=True)
os.makedirs(V)
n_old = 0
for f in sorted(os.listdir(OLD)):
    if f.endswith(".c"):
        shutil.copy(OLD + "/" + f, V + "/" + f)
        n_old += 1
assert md5(V + "/FIX.c") == md5(DEV), "FIX.c is not SEC's device"
print("CARRIED %d files from %s (FIX.c == SEC device)" % (n_old, OLD))


def put(vid, old, new):
    c = base.count(old)
    if c != 1 or old == new:
        print("ANCHOR-ERROR %s count=%d" % (vid, c))
        return
    io.open(V + "/" + vid + ".c", "w", encoding="utf-8", newline="").write(base.replace(old, new))
    print("ok %s" % vid)


#  the Opus seat's 22 (q2f), from its own generator
sys.path.insert(0, R + "/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2f/opus")
import gen_q2f  # noqa: E402
assert md5(gen_q2f.FROZEN) == md5(DEV), "Opus frozen device != SEC device"
for v in gen_q2f.variants():
    put("OQ_" + v["vid"], v["old"], v["new"])

#  3c's pass: the shapes r3d's strengthened checks are for
CLR_END = T * 4 + "INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);" + NL + T * 2 + "} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {"

    T * 4 + "INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);" + NL
    + T * 3 + "if (d->timer_control[0] && (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)) d->irq_status = 0;" + NL
    + T * 2 + "} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {")
put("R3D_CXA1", CLR_END,           # control for the k=1 row's raw-status channel: zero it at an ASSERTED asserting Clear
    T * 4 + "INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);" + NL
    + T * 3 + "if (d->irq_asserted && (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)) d->irq_status = 0;" + NL
    + T * 2 + "} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {")

    T * 3 + "odata = d->irq_status & d->irq_enable;" + NL + T * 2 + "else if (!d->warned_irq_status_w) {",

    + T * 2 + "else if (!d->warned_irq_status_w) {")

    T * 3 + "d->warned_irq_status_w = 1;" + NL,
    T * 3 + "d->warned_irq_status_w = 1;" + NL + T * 3 + "d->irq_asserted = 0;" + NL)
put("R3D_FW1",                     # deepseek: the write-data fetch reads 4 bytes whatever len is

33:  *	o)  Pretty much everything else as well :)  (This entire thing
34:  *	    is a quick hack to work primarily with NetBSD and OpenBSD
35:  *	    as guest OSes.)
36:  */
37: 
38: #include <stdio.h>
39: #include <stdlib.h>
40: #include <string.h>
41: 
42: #include "bus_pci.h"
43: #include "console.h"
44: #include "cpu.h"
45: #include "device.h"
46: #include "devices.h"
47: #include "machine.h"
48: #include "memory.h"
49: #include "misc.h"
50: #include "timer.h"
