52: #include "thirdparty/dc21285reg.h"
53: 
54: 
55: #define	DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_TICK_SHIFT	14
56: #define	DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_LENGTH		0x400
57: 
58: #define	N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS		4
59: 
60: struct footbridge_data {
61: 	struct interrupt irq;
62: 
63: 	struct pci_data *pcibus;
64: 
65: 	int		console_handle;
66: 
67: 	uint32_t	timer_load[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
68: 	uint32_t	timer_value[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
69: 	uint32_t	timer_control[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
70: 
71: 	struct interrupt timer_irq[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
72: 	struct timer	*timer[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
73: 	int		pending_timer_interrupts[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
74: 	/*  #442: the per-timer bound on the counter above, one emulated second of
75: 	    that timer's delivered rate, recomputed by reload_timer_value().  */
76: 	int		pending_bound[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
77: 
78: 	int		irq_asserted;
79: 
80: 	uint32_t	irq_status;
81: 	uint32_t	irq_enable;
82: 
83: 	uint32_t	fiq_status;
84: 	uint32_t	fiq_enable;
85: 
86: 	/*  #545: one warn-once latch per undefined-direction access that used
87: 	    to end the process (DC21285 7.3.29-7.3.33: "The Status and RawStatus
88: 	    are read-only, and EnableClear and Soft are write-only").  Zeroed by
89: 	    DEVINIT's memset and by the offline differentials' calloc.  */
90: 	int		warned_irq_status_w;
91: 	int		warned_irq_raw_w;
92: 	int		warned_irq_clear_r;
93: 	int		warned_fiq_status_w;
94: 	int		warned_fiq_raw_w;
95: };
96: 
97: 
98: /*
99:  *  #442: the counter above was incremented without a bound, so a guest that armed
100:  *  a timer and never acked overflowed it -- and on overflow the int goes negative,
101:  *  DEVICE_TICK's `> 0` is false, and THE EMULATED CLOCK DELIVERS NOTHING FOR 2^31
102:  *  FURTHER TICKS.  Reproduced at rung 3 on an unmodified -E cats: two MMIO writes,
103:  *  then 42.95 s to the wrap and a 40 s dead window, measured three times.
104:  *
105:  *  THE COMPARISON PRECEDES THE INCREMENT, and that is not a style choice.  An
106:  *  increment-then-check form -- which is what the one in-tree precedent for this
107:  *  (dev_luna88k.c:252) actually does -- reintroduces the UB it is meant to remove
108:  *  if the counter ever reaches INT_MAX, because the ++ has already executed by the
109:  *  time the check runs.  luna88k is safe only because its own reset keeps the
110:  *  counter far below INT_MAX; the FORM is still the wrong one to copy.
111:  *
112:  *  Reset to 1 rather than 0, so one interrupt is still owed -- coalescing the debt
113:  *  rather than discarding it.  MEASURED to matter: a reset-to-0 mutant survives the
114:  *  rung-3 drain probe and two of the three offline rows; only the trough row
115:  *  separates them (0 against 1).
116:  *
117:  *  NO MESSAGE FROM HERE.  These run in the SIGALRM handler and debugmsg() is
118:  *  printf underneath, so copying luna88k's diagnostic would put four printf paths
119:  *  inside a signal handler -- and timer.c:274-279 states that nothing in that file
120:  *  prints from the handler.  (luna88k's own callback does, which is a defect there;
121:  *  filed, not fixed here.)  If a diagnostic is ever wanted, latch a flag here and
122:  *  report from DEVICE_TICK, which runs in the main loop.
123:  */
124: static void footbridge_pending(struct footbridge_data *d, int nr)
125: {
126: 	if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] >= d->pending_bound[nr])
127: 		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] = 1;
128: 	else
129: 		d->pending_timer_interrupts[nr] ++;
130: }
131: 
132: static void timer_tick0(struct timer *t, void *extra)
133: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 0); }
134: static void timer_tick1(struct timer *t, void *extra)
135: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 1); }
136: static void timer_tick2(struct timer *t, void *extra)
137: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 2); }
138: static void timer_tick3(struct timer *t, void *extra)
139: { footbridge_pending((struct footbridge_data *)extra, 3); }
140: 
141: 
142: /*
143:  *  #432: ONE interpretation of the load register, used by BOTH consumers.
144:  *
145:  *  THE CRASH.  DEVICE_TICK computed `random() % timer_load[i]`, and TIMER_x_LOAD accepts a
146:  *  guest-written zero (`idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL`), so a guest could divide by zero and take the
147:  *  HOST process down -- reproduced as SIGFPE, exit 136, by two seats independently.
148:  *
149:  *  THERE ARE TWO ROUTES TO IT, and only one is ours.  Route 1: a zero load makes the rate
150:  *  +INF, which before #427/#428 meant interval 0.0 and a catch-up loop that never advanced,
151:  *  so the machine froze before this function ran; the clamp and the bound opened that path.
152:  *  ROUTE 2 PREDATES BOTH: TIMER_x_LOAD does not clear pending_timer_interrupts (TIMER_x_CONTROL
153:  *  below does), so an ORDINARY LEGAL timer accrues a backlog and a later write of zero divides
154:  *  -- measured on the pre-#427 core, it crashes there too.  DEVICE_TICK runs from machine_run(),
155:  *  the main loop, not from the signal handler, so the CPU reaches the divide between signals.
156:  *
157:  *  WHY A HELPER RATHER THAN TWO GUARDS.  Three seats converged on this independently, and the
158:  *  measuring seat REFUTED each single-site alternative: guarding only this function still
159:  *  SIGFPEs by route 2 (and is the most dangerous option precisely because it passes the obvious
160:  *  test); guarding only the modulo leaves the backlog growing 1048576 per signal, reaching
161:  *  INT_MAX in ~31 s; and normalising at the guest write corrupts read-back -- TIMER_x_LOAD
162:  *  returns the stored value, so the guest would read something it never wrote, which invents an
163:  *  answer to a question the hardware documentation we lack would have to settle.
164:  *
165:  *  THE REGISTER STAYS RAW.  timer_load[] keeps exactly what the guest wrote, masked to 24 bits.
166:  *  Only the DERIVED quantities are interpreted here, and they are model-internal.
167:  *
168:  *  ZERO MEANS FULL PERIOD (2^24), and it is a CHOICE UNDER A DOCUMENTED UNKNOWN, not a fact.
169:  *
170:  *  The 21285 datasheet was obtained for this round (_scratchpad/dc21285.pdf, Intel order
171:  *  278115-001, Sept 1998).  SS 7.3.39 (p. 7-50) specifies TimerNLoad and its reset value without
172:  *  saying what a count of zero does, and a search of the full extraction for "minimum count" /
173:  *  "nonzero" / "reaches zero" turns up no equivalent of the 8254's Figure 22.  So the absence is
174:  *  MEASURED, not assumed -- a stronger thing to say than the guess it replaces.
175:  *
176:  *  BUT "SILENT" OVERSTATES IT BY HALF, and the pass-2 review caught that in this round's favour.
177:  *  SS 7.3.41 bit 6 says a FREE-RUNNING timer "will wrap to FFFFFFh and continue to decrement",
178:  *  which together with SS 6.4's 24-bit down counter DETERMINES the free-run case: a load of zero
179:  *  gives a period of exactly 2^24, i.e. the mapping below is datasheet-IMPLIED there -- and
180:  *  free-running is bit 6 == 0, the reset mode.  It is genuinely unknown only for PERIODIC.
181:  *
182:  *  The choice therefore rests on failure asymmetry, and there are THREE candidate truths rather
183:  *  than the two first written here.  Implement full-period when the truth is "disabled" and a
184:  *  guest gets a few unwanted interrupts on a timer it explicitly enabled -- noisy, visible,
185:  *  recoverable.  Implement "disabled" when the truth is full-period and the guest waits forever
186:  *  on a tick that never comes -- silent, and it hangs at boot.  And the third, which the first
187:  *  draft omitted: read mechanically, PERIODIC mode with a zero load reloads zero and interrupts
188:  *  every prescaled clock -- a max-rate storm, the DANGEROUS branch rather than the silent one.
189:  *  Full-period is the only choice that survives all three, so the argument was understating its
190:  *  own robustness.
191:  *
192:  *  A SECOND SUPPORT WAS ALSO WITHDRAWN, for the same reason as the third and by a seat whose
193:  *  answer sat unread for a day.  It said the 24-bit mask aliases "a legitimate guest write of
194:  *  0x1000000 -- one full wrap -- to zero, so this mapping keeps the rate continuous across the
195:  *  register's own truncation".  The datasheet closes it: SS 7.3.39 gives TimerNLoad as bits 23:0
196:  *  R/W with "31:24 -- R Read only as 0", so bit 24 is not a truncated write at all, it is OUTSIDE
197:  *  THE WRITABLE FIELD.  There is no wrap to be continuous across, and continuity beyond a
198:  *  register's own width is a modelling preference dressed up as arithmetic.  Zero -> 2^24 stands
199:  *  on the three-way argument above and on nothing else; what silicon does with a zero load
200:  *  remains UNKNOWN, and SS 7.3.39 is silent on it.
201:  *
202:  *  A THIRD ARGUMENT WAS WITHDRAWN WHEN THE DATASHEET ARRIVED, and it is recorded because the
203:  *  withdrawal matters more than the argument did.  An earlier draft of this comment said
204:  *  "devinit resets every load to TIMER_MAX_VAL, so the model's own convention is full width".
205:  *  The datasheet says TimerNLoad and TimerNControl BOTH reset to 0 (SS 7.3.39, SS 7.3.41), so
206:  *  this model's reset values are themselves wrong -- that line was citing a model bug as
207:  *  evidence.  The divergence is filed; it is not fixed here.
208:  *
209:  *  THE WIDTH FIX IS NOT SEPARABLE FROM THE ZERO FIX.  The old `int cycles` with `cycles <<= 8`
210:  *  overflowed for exactly half the legal 24-bit range (first bad load 0x800000), yielding a
211:  *  NEGATIVE frequency that the core's floor clamp silently turned into one tick per ~3.2 years
212:  *  -- reachable with NO load write at all, since the reset load is TIMER_MAX_VAL and a single
213:  *  write of ENABLE|FCLK_256 gets there.  And mapping zero to 2^24 while leaving `int` gives
214:  *  2^24 << 8 == 2^32 == 0, which is +INF AGAIN: the obvious zero-guard RE-CREATES the bug it
215:  *  removes.  Hence uint64_t, and multiplication rather than a signed shift.  Found independently
216:  *  by three seats.
217:  *
218:  *  (8253 maps a zero divisor to "disabled" instead -- and a pass-2 seat caught that an earlier
219:  *  version of this parenthetical argued the point BACKWARDS.  It said the 8253's 16-bit counter
220:  *  "has no equivalent of the 24-bit aliasing above", but writing 0x10000 to a 16-bit latch aliases
221:  *  to zero in exactly the same way -- which is precisely WHY the Intel 8254 datasheet defines a
222:  *  programmed count of 0 as 2^16 (231164-005, p. 17).  So the aliasing argument REINFORCES this
223:  *  mapping rather than distinguishing it, and dev_8253.c's "disabled" is the one that looks wrong
224:  *  against its own hardware.  Filed as its own item; not changed here.)
225:  */
226: /*
227:  *  with_prescaler distinguishes the two consumers, and it is NOT a convenience flag -- getting
228:  *  it wrong is a defect the measuring seat caught in its own first proposal.  The prescaler
229:  *  slows the RATE; it does not widen the COUNTER.  Feeding the prescaled period to the modulo
230:  *  put values up to 0x7fffca11 into a 24-bit register that the guest can read back -- measured,
231:  *  19833 of 20000 ticks out of range.  So: the rate consumer prescales, the counter consumer
232:  *  does not, and both get their never-zero span from this one function.
233:  */
234: static uint64_t footbridge_effective_cycles(uint32_t load, uint32_t control,
235: 	int with_prescaler)
236: {
237: 	uint64_t cycles = load;
238: 
239: 	if (cycles == 0)
240: 		cycles = (uint64_t)TIMER_MAX_VAL + 1;	/*  0x1000000 == 2^24  */
241: 
242: 	if (!with_prescaler)
243: 		return cycles;
244: 
245: 	/*  The prescaler scales the PERIOD.  It must not be a signed shift, and the order
246: 	    matters: TIMER_EXTERNAL is FCLK_16|FCLK_256, so test the pair first or 0x0C reads
247: 	    as a bare FCLK_16 by luck of the `else if`.  */
248: 	if ((control & (TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256))
249: 	    == (TIMER_FCLK_16 | TIMER_FCLK_256)) {
250: 		/*  TIMER_EXTERNAL (0x0C): a LEGAL encoding, and this is now DATASHEET-BACKED
251: 		    rather than inferred from the header.  The 21285 datasheet obtained for this
252: 		    round gives TimerNControl bits 3:2 as 00=fclk_in, 01=fclk_in/16,
253: 		    10=fclk_in/256, 11=External event (SS 7.3.41, p. 7-51; SS 6.4, p. 6-11 lists
254: 		    the same four).  It used to reach fatal()+exit(1) below, so a CONFORMING guest
255: 		    selecting the external clock source killed the host -- the same defect class as
256: 		    the divide and arguably likelier, being a documented mode rather than a
257: 		    malformed value.  The RATE an external source would give depends on a pin this
258: 		    model has no signal for (irq_in_l), so it is genuinely not derivable here and
259: 		    is NOT invented: the unprescaled period keeps the timer sane and the emulator
260: 		    alive.  Modelling the external event properly is filed.  */
261: 		;
262: 	} else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_16)
263: 		cycles *= 16;
264: 	else if (control & TIMER_FCLK_256)
265: 		cycles *= 256;
266: 
267: 	return cycles;
268: }
269: 
270: 
271: static void reload_timer_value(struct cpu *cpu, struct footbridge_data *d,
272: 	int timer_nr)
273: {
274: 	double freq = (double)cpu->machine->emulated_hz;
275: 
276: 	freq /= (double) footbridge_effective_cycles(d->timer_load[timer_nr],
277: 	    d->timer_control[timer_nr], 1);
278: 
279: 	d->timer_value[timer_nr] = d->timer_load[timer_nr];
280: 
281: 	/*
282: 	 *  #442: one emulated second of THIS timer's delivered rate, clamped.
283: 	 *
284: 	 *  The ceiling is DERIVED, not chosen: TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP ticks per timer
285: 	 *  per signal at TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY signals per second is the most the
286: 	 *  timer core can ever deliver to one timer in one wall second, so a cap
287: 	 *  at that value can never throttle a legitimately fast timer.
288: 	 *
289: 	 *  IT IS ALSO NOT OPTIONAL, which a purely rate-relative bound hides.
290: 	 *  MEASURED: with `-I 2147483647` and load 1 the requested rate is
291: 	 *  2147483647.0, so (int)freq is INT_MAX and a bound of "the timer's own
292: 	 *  rate" never fires -- the overflow returns, reachable through a
293: 	 *  documented command-line flag.  Deleting these two lines is the
294: 	 *  smallest edit that still passes the rung-3 probe.
295: 	 *
296: 	 *  Floor 1.  *** ITS ORIGINAL JUSTIFICATION WAS MEASURED FALSE and is
297: 	 *  corrected here rather than quietly dropped: *** the comment claimed a
298: 	 *  0.0116 Hz timer "would otherwise bound at 0 and the counter would be
299: 	 *  pinned there".  With the floor PRESENT that case already reads peak 1,
300: 	 *  and with the floor DELETED the harness output is BYTE-IDENTICAL -- a
301: 	 *  bound of 0 and a bound of 1 both pin at 1.  The floor prevents nothing
302: 	 *  today; it is kept as a guard against a future bound expression that
303: 	 *  could go negative, and that is the honest reason.
304: 	 *
305: 	 *  The comparisons are negated, but NOT for the reason first written.
306: 	 *  timer_clamp_freq() puts its FLOOR first deliberately (timer.c:80-82:
307: 	 *  "the FLOOR is tested FIRST so a NaN lands SLOW rather than fast"), and
308: 	 *  this tests the CEILING first -- the inverse.  Harmless, because NaN is
309: 	 *  UNREACHABLE here: emulated_hz is an int (machine.h:191) and
310: 	 *  footbridge_effective_cycles() is >= 1 by construction, so this is
311: 	 *  finite / finite-nonzero.  The claim to "match timer_clamp_freq's idiom"
312: 	 *  was false in the one respect that idiom is about.
313: 	 *
314: 	 *  Set before timer_add()/timer_update_frequency() below.  *** THE REASON
315: 	 *  FIRST GIVEN HERE -- that a SIGALRM landing between them would find a
316: 	 *  zero bound -- IS NOT LOAD-BEARING, measured. ***  On the FIRST arm the
317: 	 *  timer is not in first_timer until timer_add() links it, and timer.c
318: 	 *  publishes fully-initialised (next stored before first_timer); on later
319: 	 *  arms the OLD bound is still a valid bound.  A mutant moving this store
320: 	 *  below the switch is a ZERO-BYTE object delta and passes every row.
321: 	 *
322: 	 *  Kept in this order because it reads correctly and costs nothing, not
323: 	 *  because it closes a window.  Note also that this and the DEVINIT seed
324: 	 *  are REDUNDANT WITH EACH OTHER -- either alone removes the zero-bound
325: 	 *  state -- and neither comment used to mention the other doing the work.
326: 	 */
327: 	{
328: 		double bound = freq;
329: 		if (!(bound <= (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY))
330: 			bound = (double)TIMER_MAX_CATCHUP * TIMER_BASE_FREQUENCY;
331: 		if (!(bound >= 1.0))
332: 			bound = 1.0;
333: 		d->pending_bound[timer_nr] = (int) bound;
334: 	}
335: 
336: 	/*  printf("%i: %i -> %f Hz\n", timer_nr,
337: 	    d->timer_load[timer_nr], freq);  */
338: 
339: 	if (d->timer[timer_nr] == NULL) {
340: 		switch (timer_nr) {
341: 		case 0:	d->timer[0] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick0, d); break;
342: 		case 1:	d->timer[1] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick1, d); break;
343: 		case 2:	d->timer[2] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick2, d); break;
344: 		case 3:	d->timer[3] = timer_add(freq, timer_tick3, d); break;
345: 		}
346: 	} else {
347: 		timer_update_frequency(d->timer[timer_nr], freq);
348: 	}
349: }
350: 
351: 
352: /*
353:  *  The 4 footbridge timers should decrease and cause interrupts. Periodic
354:  *  interrupts restart as soon as they are acknowledged, non-periodic
355:  *  interrupts need to be "reloaded" to restart.
356:  *
357:  *  TODO: Hm. I thought I had solved this, but it didn't quite work.
358:  *        This needs to be re-checked against documentation, sometime.
359:  */
360: DEVICE_TICK(footbridge)
361: {
362: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
363: 	int i;
364: 
365: 	for (i=0; i<N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
366: 		if (d->timer_control[i] & TIMER_ENABLE) {
367: 			if (d->pending_timer_interrupts[i] > 0) {
368: 				/*  #432: the same never-zero span, WITHOUT the
369: 				    prescaler -- this is a 24-bit counter, and the
370: 				    prescaler slows the rate rather than widening

794: 	/*
795: 	 *  #442: the memset leaves pending_bound[] at 0, and a bound of 0 would pin

797: 	 *  reload_timer_value() sets the bound, and it is also the only thing that

809: 	 *  pre-fix.  If the scenario this seed exists for ever happened, it would
810: 	 *  hand that timer the LARGEST bound the fix permits.  1 can only
811: 	 *  under-deliver, which is the right direction for a value that should
812: 	 *  never be used.
813: 	 */
814: 	for (i = 0; i < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++)
815: 		d->pending_bound[i] = 1;
816: 
817: 	/*  Connect to the CPU which this footbridge will interrupt:  */
818: 	INTERRUPT_CONNECT(devinit->interrupt_path, d->irq);
819: 
820: 	/*  DC21285 register access:  */
821: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, devinit->name,
822: 	    devinit->addr, DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_LENGTH,
823: 	    dev_footbridge_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
824: 
825: 	/*  ISA interrupt status/acknowledgement:  */
826: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "footbridge_isa",
827: 	    0x79000000, 8, dev_footbridge_isa_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
828: 
829: 	/*  The "fcom" console:  */
830: 	d->console_handle = console_start_slave(devinit->machine, "fcom", 0);
831: 
832: 	/*  Register 32 footbridge interrupts:  */
833: 	snprintf(irq_path, sizeof(irq_path), "%s.footbridge",
834: 	    devinit->interrupt_path);
835: 	for (i=0; i<32; i++) {
836: 		struct interrupt interrupt_template;
837: 		char tmpstr[600];
838: 
839: 		memset(&interrupt_template, 0, sizeof(interrupt_template));
840: 		interrupt_template.line = (uint32_t)1 << i;
841: 		snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "%s.%i", irq_path, i);
842: 		interrupt_template.name = tmpstr;
843: 
844: 		interrupt_template.extra = d;
845: 		interrupt_template.interrupt_assert =
846: 		    footbridge_interrupt_assert;
847: 		interrupt_template.interrupt_deassert =
848: 		    footbridge_interrupt_deassert;
849: 		interrupt_handler_register(&interrupt_template);
850: 
851: 		/*  Connect locally to some interrupts:  */
852: 		if (i>=IRQ_TIMER_1 && i<=IRQ_TIMER_4)
853: 			INTERRUPT_CONNECT(tmpstr, d->timer_irq[i-IRQ_TIMER_1]);
854: 	}
855: 
856: 	switch (devinit->machine->machine_type) {
857: 	case MACHINE_CATS:
858: 		snprintf(irq_path_isa, sizeof(irq_path_isa), "%s.10", irq_path);
859: 		break;
860: 	case MACHINE_NETWINDER:
861: 		snprintf(irq_path_isa, sizeof(irq_path_isa), "%s.11", irq_path);
862: 		break;
863: 	default:fatal("footbridge unimpl machine type\n");
864: 		exit(1);
865: 	}
866: 
867: 	/*  A PCI bus:  */
868: 	d->pcibus = bus_pci_init(
869: 	    devinit->machine,
870: 	    irq_path,
871: 	    0x7c000000,		/*  PCI device io offset  */
872: 	    0x80000000,		/*  PCI device mem offset  */
873: 	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI port base  */
874: 	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI mem base  */
875: 	    irq_path,		/*  PCI irq base  */
876: 	    0x7c000000,		/*  ISA port base  */
877: 	    0x80000000,		/*  ISA mem base  */
878: 	    irq_path_isa);	/*  ISA port base  */
879: 
880: 	/*  ... with some default devices for known machine types:  */
881: 	switch (devinit->machine->machine_type) {
882: 	case MACHINE_CATS:
883: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
884: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 7, 0, "ali_m1543");
885: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
886: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 10, 0, "dec21143");
887: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
888: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 16, 0, "ali_m5229");
889: 		break;
890: 	case MACHINE_NETWINDER:
891: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
892: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 11, 0, "symphony_83c553");
893: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
894: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 11, 1, "symphony_82c105");
895: 		memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory,
896: 		    "footbridge_reset", 0x7c000338, 1,
897: 		    dev_footbridge_reset_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
898: 		break;
899: 	default:fatal("footbridge: unimplemented machine type.\n");
900: 		exit(1);
901: 	}
902: 
903: 	/*  PCI configuration space:  */
904: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory,
905: 	    "footbridge_pci", pci_addr, 0x1000000,
906: 	    dev_footbridge_pci_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
907: 
908: 	/*  Timer ticks:  */
909: 	for (i=0; i<N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
910: 		d->timer_control[i] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC;
911: 		d->timer_load[i] = TIMER_MAX_VAL;
912: 	}
913: 
914: 	machine_add_tickfunction(devinit->machine,
915: 	    dev_footbridge_tick, d, DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_TICK_SHIFT);
916: 
917: 	devinit->return_ptr = d->pcibus;
918: 	return 1;
919: }

WHAT 3d CHANGES (diff_footbridge.c 8309c5b7 -> the r3d copy; no row added, 864 stays):
  E1  "X14 %s: Clear 0x20 keeps 0x10 pending and enabled" (x2, both timer states): + x->irq_status == 0x18
  E2  "X14 line 4 raised by a device: IRQ_STATUS reads 0x10": + x->fiq_status == 0 && x->fiq_enable == 0
  E3  "X14 line 4 raised by a device: Clear %s, CPU line %s once" (x2) renamed "... Clear %s, the raw status kept,
      CPU line %s once": + x->irq_status == 0x10.  irq_asserted deliberately NOT pinned (see GUARD/BODY below).
  E4  "X14 line 4 ...: 5 declines, then the legal reads and a Clear re-arm no latch": + x->irq_asserted == 1
      && x->fiq_status == 0 && x->fiq_enable == 0
  E5  X12/X13: memset(b, 0xa5, sizeof b) before each Clear write, so the bytes above the width are not zero
  and the contract's records (COVERED / NOT COVERED / headers / IDENTITY comment).

1. SIBLING PARITY
   - E1's sibling is the DEVINIT "Set 0x40 then Clear 0x10" row on the same instance kind: its raw status is read by
     the declines row that follows on that instance (x->irq_status == 0x18) -- parity holds without a change there.
   - E3's two rows are siblings of each other (k = 0 deasserting, k = 1 asserting): both gain the raw status.  The re-arm
     row (a third asserted Clear, 0x400) already read it (0x10) -- parity now holds across all three asserted Clears.
   - E2's sibling is the re-arm row, which also reads IRQ_STATUS while irq_asserted: E4 gives it the FIQ fields too.
   - The DEVINIT declines row already reads the four fields; the asserted declines (re-arm row) now read irq_asserted
     and the FIQ fields as well (E4).  The DEVINIT declines cannot show an irq_asserted store: it is 0 there.
   - E5: X12 and X13 are the only rows with a narrow legal write (the Clear); both get the fill.  X6's narrow writes are
     declines, whose bodies never read idata -- N/A there, reason recorded.

2. CLAIM -> ROW (each added sentence asserting an observable)
   - "the raw status stays 0x18, as the name says"                -> E1 rows (mutant R3D_CXC1)
   - "a Clear writes the enable only -- the raw status is the sources'" -> E3 rows (OQ_FS01; R3D_CXA1 at k = 1)
   - "irq_asserted is NOT pinned here ... a row pinning it would pin that defect" -> NO ROW, by design: the enable arms
     never write irq_asserted (fbirqstuck, F13); pinning 1 after a deasserting Clear pins the defect, pinning 0 is false
     of today's code.  The Opus seat's FS02 stays in NOT COVERED's LEVEL line.
   - COVERED "(the value, the five fields ... and the output)"    -> E2 row (R3D_GF1)
   - COVERED "5 announces, one CPU assert, the fields and irq_asserted kept" -> E4 row (R3D_DA1, R3D_GF1)
   - COVERED/X12 "the buffer's bytes above the width not zero"    -> X12/X13 narrow rows (R3D_FW1)
   - NOT COVERED additions -- no row, by design, each a clause-(e) disclosure measured in this table: the CALLER
     context (OQ_MC01-05, OQ_MC07), the announce TEXT (OQ_TX01), a latch's HISTORY (R3D_LH1), FS02 (OQ_FS02).
   - NOT COVERED corrections (records only): the exact-value example (the Opus seat's measured forms), bit 16 of the
     unraised 25 (N_SB16 red, carried), the producer pair's text and its filing, F1 on 864, THE SPACE's rest of the

3. GUARD FIELD vs BODY FIELD
   N/A for new guards: 3d changes no product line and no guard; #545's guards and bodies are unchanged since r3.
   Applied instead to the ROWS: each new conjunct reads a field the model writes on the path the row drives --

     fiq_status / fiq_enable: fiq_status has no writer; fiq_enable only by the FIQ enable arms -> constant (0) on the

381: 
382: /*
383:  *  footbridge_interrupt_assert():
384:  */
385: void footbridge_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
386: {
387: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
388: 	d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;
389: 
390: 	// Patch from NetBSD:
391: 	// "fix hang interrupt issue. Always assert the irq's even if we have
392: 	//  already asserted the global irq for the device. prevents hangs with
393: 	//  cats."
394: 	if ((d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) /* && !d->irq_asserted */) {
395: 		d->irq_asserted = 1;
396: 		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
397: 	}
398: }
399: 
400: 
401: /*
402:  *  footbridge_interrupt_deassert():
403:  */
404: void footbridge_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
405: {
406: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
407: 	d->irq_status &= ~interrupt->line;
408: 
409: 	if (!(d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) && d->irq_asserted) {
410: 		d->irq_asserted = 0;
411: 		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
412: 	}
413: }
414: 
415: 
416: /*
417:  *  Reading the byte at 0x79000000 is a quicker way to figure out which ISA
418:  *  interrupt has occurred (and acknowledging it at the same time), than
419:  *  dealing with the legacy 0x20/0xa0 ISA ports.
420:  */
421: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_isa)
422: {
423: 	/*  struct footbridge_data *d = extra;  */
424: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
425: 	int x;
426: 
427: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
428: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
429: 		fatal("[ footbridge_isa: WARNING/TODO: write! idata = 0x%016" PRIx64" ]\n", idata);
430: 	}
431: 
432: 	x = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.last_int;
433: 	if (x < 8)
434: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic1->irq_base + x;
435: 	else
436: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic2->irq_base + x - 8;
437: 
438: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
439: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
440: 
441: 	return 1;
442: }
443: 
444: 
445: /*
446:  *  Reset pin at ISA port 0x338, at least in the NetWinder:
447:  *
448:  *  TODO: NOT WORKING YET!
449:  */
450: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_reset)
451: {
452: 	uint64_t idata = 0;
453: 
454: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
455: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
456: 		if (idata & 0x40) {
457: 			debug("[ footbridge_reset: GP16: Halting. ]\n");
458: 			/*  #220: (Codex/Fable) cpu->running=0 already halts cleanly;
459: 			    the col-0 exit(1) was a debug hack that kills the host.  */
460: 			cpu->running = 0;
461: 		}
462: 	}
463: 
464: 	return 1;
465: }
466: 
467: 
468: /*
469:  *  The Footbridge PCI configuration space is implemented as a direct memory
470:  *  space (i.e. not one port for addr and one port for data). This function
471:  *  translates that into bus_pci calls.
472:  */
473: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_pci)
474: {
475: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
476: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
477: 	int bus, dev, func, reg;
478: 
479: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
480: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len|MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN);
481: 
482: 	/*  Decompose the (direct) address into its components:  */
483: 	bus_pci_decompose_1(relative_addr, &bus, &dev, &func, &reg);
484: 	bus_pci_setaddr(cpu, d->pcibus, bus, dev, func, reg);
485: 
486: 	if (bus == 255) {
487: 		fatal("[ footbridge DEBUG ERROR: bus 255 unlikely,"
488: 		    " pc (might not be updated) = 0x%08x ]\n", (int)cpu->pc);
489: 		/*  #220: (Codex/Fable) don't exit() the host on a guest PCI
490: 		    access to the (unlikely) bus 255; treat it as handled.  */
491: 		return 1;
492: 	}
493: 
494: 	debug("[ footbridge pci: %s bus %i, device %i, function %i, register "
495: 	    "%i ]\n", writeflag == MEM_READ? "read from" : "write to", bus,
496: 	    dev, func, reg);
497: 
498: 	bus_pci_data_access(cpu, d->pcibus, writeflag == MEM_READ?
499: 	    &odata : &idata, len, writeflag);
500: 
501: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
502: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len|MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN, odata);
503: 
504: 	return 1;
505: }
506: 
507: 
508: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)
509: {
510: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
511: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
512: 	int timer_nr = 0;
513: 
514: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
515: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
516: 
517: 	if (relative_addr >= TIMER_1_LOAD && relative_addr <= TIMER_4_CLEAR) {
518: 		timer_nr = (relative_addr >> 5) & (N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS - 1);
519: 		relative_addr &= ~0x060;
520: 	}
521: 
522: 	switch (relative_addr) {
523: 
524: 	case VENDOR_ID:
525: 		odata = 0x1011;  /*  DC21285_VENDOR_ID  */
526: 		break;
527: 
528: 	case DEVICE_ID:
529: 		odata = 0x1065;  /*  DC21285_DEVICE_ID  */
530: 		break;
531: 
532: 	case 0x04:
533: 	case 0x0c:
534: 	case 0x10:
535: 	case 0x14:
536: 	case 0x18:
537: 		/*  TODO. Written to by Linux.  */
538: 		break;
539: 
540: 	case REVISION:
541: 		odata = 3;  /*  footbridge revision number  */
542: 		break;
543: 
544: 	case PCI_ADDRESS_EXTENSION:
545: 		/*  TODO: Written to by Linux.  */
546: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
547: 			fatal("[ footbridge: TODO: write to PCI_ADDRESS_"
548: 			    "EXTENSION: 0x%llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
549: 		break;
550: 
551: 	case SA_CONTROL:
552: 		/*  Read by Linux:  */
553: 		odata = PCI_CENTRAL_FUNCTION;
554: 		break;
555: 
556: 	case UART_DATA:
557: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
558: 			console_putchar(d->console_handle, idata);
559: 		break;
560: 
561: 	case UART_RX_STAT:
562: 		/*  TODO  */
563: 		odata = 0;
564: 		break;
565: 
566: 	case UART_FLAGS:
567: 		odata = UART_TX_EMPTY;
568: 		break;
569: 
570: 	case IRQ_STATUS:
571: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
572: 			odata = d->irq_status & d->irq_enable;
573: 		else if (!d->warned_irq_status_w) {
574: 			/*  #545: read-only register (7.3.29); the write is
575: 			    declined -- no register changes, only this site's
576: 			    warn-once latch is set -- and announced once.  */
577: 			d->warned_irq_status_w = 1;
578: 			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to irq status? "
579: 			    "(once) ]\n");
580: 		}
581: 		break;
582: 
583: 	case IRQ_RAW_STATUS:
584: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
585: 			odata = d->irq_status;
586: 		else if (!d->warned_irq_raw_w) {
587: 			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.30); declined, announced once.  */
588: 			d->warned_irq_raw_w = 1;
589: 			fatal("[ footbridge write to irq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
590: 		}
591: 		break;
592: 
593: 	case IRQ_ENABLE_SET:
594: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
595: 			d->irq_enable |= idata;
596: 			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
597: 				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
598: 			else
599: 				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
600: 		} else {
601: 			/*  #539: a read of 188h is documented -- IRQEnable is
602: 			    "read-only ... can only be changed by writing to
603: 			    the EnableSet and EnableClear registers", reset 0
604: 			    (DC21285 Table 7-10, 7.3.31).  The value below was
605: 			    always right; the exit(1) that followed it is gone.  */
606: 			odata = d->irq_enable;
607: 		}
608: 		break;
609: 
610: 	case IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR:
611: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
612: 			d->irq_enable &= ~idata;
613: 			if (d->irq_status & d->irq_enable)
614: 				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
615: 			else
616: 				INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
617: 		} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {
618: 			/*  #545: IRQEnableClear is write-only (7.3.33) and Table
619: 			    7-10 names no register at 18Ch for reads, so the read
620: 			    is declined with 0 -- the IRQEnable value that used to
621: 			    be computed here is 188h's answer, not 18Ch's.  */
622: 			d->warned_irq_clear_r = 1;
623: 			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge read from "
624: 			    "ENABLE CLEAR? (once) ]\n");
625: 		}
626: 		break;
627: 
628: 	case FIQ_STATUS:
629: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
630: 			odata = d->fiq_status & d->fiq_enable;
631: 		else if (!d->warned_fiq_status_w) {
632: 			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.29); declined, announced once.  */
633: 			d->warned_fiq_status_w = 1;
634: 			fatal("[ WARNING: footbridge write to fiq status? "
635: 			    "(once) ]\n");
636: 		}
637: 		break;
638: 
639: 	case FIQ_RAW_STATUS:
640: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
641: 			odata = d->fiq_status;
642: 		else if (!d->warned_fiq_raw_w) {
643: 			/*  #545: read-only (7.3.30); declined, announced once.  */
644: 			d->warned_fiq_raw_w = 1;
645: 			fatal("[ footbridge write to fiq_raw_status (once) ]\n");
646: 		}

688: 			    HOST.  Arguably likelier than the zero load, since it
689: 			    is a documented mode rather than a malformed value.

691: 			    UNKNOWN and is NOT invented: footbridge_effective_cycles()
692: 			    now gives the encoding a defined, survivable meaning
693: 			    (the unprescaled period), and the real semantics are
694: 			    filed for a round that has a datasheet.  */

708: 		    pending count and DEASSERTED the interrupt.  A guest that merely
709: 		    read the register silently acknowledged a pending timer interrupt;
710: 		    the guest-visible consequence is a LOST interrupt, not a bad value.
711: 
712: 		    FOUR addresses, not one: the fold above normalises 0x30C/0x32C/
713: 		    0x34C/0x36C onto this single case.
714: 
715: 		    GUARD THE BODY, KEEP THE break.  An early return here would skip

717: 		    would hand a guest an untouched buffer -- a defect this code does
718: 		    not currently have.  */

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Opus seat, #545 repair-3c pass (q2f): NEW one-line shapes, scored on the frozen 864-row instrument.

Each carries a PREDICTION written before any run: RED; EQ (green, equivalent); GREEN-DISC (green, a real change the
864-row contract's NOT COVERED list names); GREEN-REAL (green, a real change NOT named there -- a finding if measured so).
`target` names the row(s) a RED is predicted on, where that is the point of the shape.
Every `old` must occur exactly once in the frozen device (check_anchors()).
"""
import io, sys

FROZEN = "/mnt/c/DocumentNoSnc/cc/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/fbd5/q2f/frozen/dev_footbridge.c"
T, NL = "\t", "\n"

               read_old=T * 3 + "odata = d->irq_status & d->irq_enable;"),

               read_old=T * 3 + "odata = d->irq_status;" + NL + T * 2 + "else if (!d->warned_irq_raw_w) {"),
    "A2": dict(dir_old=T * 2 + "if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {" + NL + T * 3 + "d->irq_enable &= ~idata;",
               flag_old=T * 2 + "} else if (!d->warned_irq_clear_r) {", body_old=T * 3 + "d->warned_irq_clear_r = 1;"),
    "A3": dict(dir_old=T * 2 + "if (writeflag == MEM_READ)" + NL + T * 3 + "odata = d->fiq_status & d->fiq_enable;",

V = []


def add(vid, fam, arm, old, new, text, pred, target="", note=""):
    V.append(dict(vid=vid, family=fam, arm=arm, old=old, new=new, text=text, pred=pred, target=target, note=note))


def first_line(s):
    return s.split(NL)[0]


def dir_(vid, fam, arm, expr, pred, target="", note=""):
    a = ARMS[arm]
    fl = first_line(a["dir_old"])
    tail = " {" if fl.endswith("{") else ""
    add(vid, fam, arm, a["dir_old"], a["dir_old"].replace(fl, T * 2 + "if (" + expr + ")" + tail, 1),
        "if (" + expr + ")" + tail, pred, target, note)


def flag_(vid, fam, arm, expr, pred, target="", note=""):
    a = ARMS[arm]
    nl = T * 2 + ("} else if (" if arm == "A2" else "else if (") + expr + ") {"
    add(vid, fam, arm, a["flag_old"], nl, nl.strip(), pred, target, note)


def body_(vid, fam, arm, stmt, pred, target="", note=""):
    add(vid, fam, arm, ARMS[arm]["body_old"], T * 3 + stmt, stmt, pred, target, note)


def read_(vid, fam, arm, stmt, pred, target="", note=""):
    old = ARMS[arm]["read_old"]
    add(vid, fam, arm, old, old.replace(first_line(old), T * 3 + stmt, 1), stmt, pred, target, note)


def clr_(vid, fam, idx, line, pred, target="", note=""):
    L = list(CLR)
    L[idx] = line
    add(vid, fam, "A2", CLR_OLD, NL.join(L) + CLR_TAIL, line.strip(), pred, target, note)


def clr_after(vid, fam, line, pred, target="", note=""):
    """insert ONE line after the Clear write's drive (after `INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);`)"""
    add(vid, fam, "A2", CLR_OLD, NL.join(CLR) + NL + line + CLR_TAIL, line.strip(), pred, target, note)


def fetch_(vid, fam, expr, pred, target="", note=""):
    add(vid, fam, "head", IDL, IDL.replace(T + "if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)", T + "if (" + expr + ")", 1),
        "if (" + expr + ") idata = memory_readmax64(...)", pred, target, note)

def variants():
    ids = [v["vid"] for v in V]
    assert len(ids) == len(set(ids)), "duplicate ids"
    return V


def check_anchors(allv):
    fz = io.open(FROZEN, encoding="utf-8", newline="").read()
    return [(v["vid"], fz.count(v["old"])) for v in allv if fz.count(v["old"]) != 1 or v["old"] == v["new"]]

void fatal(const char *fmt, ...)
{
	va_list argp;

	va_start(argp, fmt);
	va_debug(argp, fmt);
	va_end(argp);
}


void debugmsg_set_verbosity_level(int subsystem, int verbosity)
{
	if (subsystem != SUBSYS_ALL)
		debugmsg_current_verbosity[subsystem] = verbosity;
	else
		for (size_t i = 0; i < debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems; ++i)
			debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] = verbosity;
}


void debugmsg_add_verbosity_level(int subsystem, int verbosity_delta)
{
	if (subsystem != SUBSYS_ALL)
		debugmsg_current_verbosity[subsystem] += verbosity_delta;
	else
		for (size_t i = 0; i < debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems; ++i)
			debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] += verbosity_delta;

	for (size_t i = 0; i < debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems; ++i)
		if (debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] < 0)
			debugmsg_current_verbosity[i] = 0;
}


/*
 *  debugmsg_register_subsystem():
 *
 *  Registers a new subsystem.
 */
int debugmsg_register_subsystem(const char* name)
{
	// TODO: Another way would be to support multiple names with e.g.
	// a suffix. Such as "le0", "le1", "le2" for the name "le".
	// But for now, simply return the same subsystem for all of them.
	ssize_t subsys = -1;
	for (size_t i = 0; i < debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems; ++i)
		if (strcmp(name, debugmsg_subsystem_name[i]) == 0)
			subsys = i;

	if (subsys >= 0)
		return subsys;
	
	subsys = debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems;

	debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems ++;
	debugmsg_subsystem_name = realloc(debugmsg_subsystem_name,
	    sizeof(char*) * debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems);
	debugmsg_current_verbosity = realloc(debugmsg_current_verbosity,
	    sizeof(int) * debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems);;
	debugmsg_breakpoint_level = realloc(debugmsg_breakpoint_level,
	    sizeof(int) * debugmsg_nr_of_subsystems);

	debugmsg_subsystem_name[subsys] = strdup(name);
	debugmsg_breakpoint_level[subsys] = -1;
	debugmsg_set_verbosity_level(subsys, default_verbosity);

	return subsys;
}


/*
 *  debugmsg_change_settings():
 *
 *  Changes the current verbosity level settings for a given setting name.
 */
void debugmsg_change_settings(char *subsystem_name, char *n)
    #  regress/artifacts/fbd5/q2_20260923/{repair,repair2,r3_20260923}/.
    #  COUNTED AS DISTINCT GREEN ROW NAMES, not green lines (adjudication pass, G10): a duplicated green
    #  line cannot stand in for a missing row.  Every row name in the file is unique on the fix.
    fb_distinct() { grep -E "^  ok    $1 " "$FBLOG" | sed -E 's/^  ok    //; s/[[:space:]]+[0-9]+$//' | sort -u | wc -l | tr -d ' '; }
    check "footbridge: #545 P-rows green (P0-P9), distinct names" "$(fb_distinct 'P[0-9]')" "78"
    check "footbridge: #545 X-rows green (X1-X8), distinct names" "$(fb_distinct 'X[1-8]')" "440"
    check "footbridge: #545 X6 every declined-access cell green" "$(fb_distinct 'X6')" "248"
    check "footbridge: #545 X7 every sampled legal access re-arms no latch" "$(fb_distinct 'X7')" "148"
    check "footbridge: #545 X8 wide legal reads keep all 32 bits" "$(fb_distinct 'X8')" "6"
    check "footbridge: #545 X9 legal access, latches clear, all states" "$(fb_distinct 'X9')" "100"
    check "footbridge: #545 X10 legal access, latches SET, value kept" "$(fb_distinct 'X10')" "72"
    check "footbridge: #545 X11 every ordered pair of site latches, four states" "$(fb_distinct 'X11')" "80"
    check "footbridge: #545 X12 legal Clear write, every width and data" "$(fb_distinct 'X12')" "20"
    check "footbridge: #545 X13 legal Clear write, latches SET" "$(fb_distinct 'X13')" "20"
    check "footbridge: #545 X14 instance cells (drive test, DEVINIT, irq_asserted)" "$(fb_distinct 'X14')" "21"
    #  Name each row that is the SOLE detector of something.
    check "footbridge: the zero-load row is present" \
          "$(grep -c 'load 0 becomes the full 24-bit span' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: the prescaler-overflow rows are present" \
          "$(grep -c 'does not overflow\|does not wrap to zero' "$FBLOG")" "2"
    check "footbridge: the TIMER_EXTERNAL row is present" \
          "$(grep -c 'not silently read as x16' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    #  #474: the five fbclearread rows.  Kill table 5/5 in
    #  regress/artifacts/fbclearread/killtable_20260827/.  The last three exist ONLY
    #  because the pass-2 codex seat returned DO NOT SHIP on a detector that was already
    #  green: it named an early-`return` mutant (the very defect the fix comment claims to
    #  avoid, and nothing tested it) and a guard widened to admit a PERIODIC read.  Both
    #  were built and both were killed by these rows.
    check "footbridge: the fbclearread defect row is present" \
          "$(grep -c 'a READ of TIMER_1_CLEAR must NOT clear pending' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    #  *** grep -cF, NOT grep -c: `[LIVE]` is a BRACKET EXPRESSION in a basic
    #  regex, so `grep -c '[LIVE] a WRITE...'` looks for ONE of L/I/V/E followed
    #  by ' a WRITE...' and can NEVER match a line whose label ends in ']'.
    #  Both rows were PRESENT and GREEN while these two checks reported got=0,
    #  so the offline gate was red from #474's second wave and the redness was
    #  attributable to nothing.  A CHECK THAT CANNOT PASS -- the inverse of the
    #  carrier's check-that-cannot-fail, and louder, but still a wrong record.
    #  Sibling sweep at the time of the fix: 5 bracket patterns among 175
    #  `grep -c` invocations across the gates; the other 3 (`^[XY]`, `^[<>]`)
    #  are deliberate character classes and were left alone.
    check "footbridge: the fbclearread live control is present" \
          "$(grep -cF '[LIVE] a WRITE of TIMER_1_CLEAR decrements pending' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: the common-tail row is present" \
          "$(grep -c 'a READ still runs the common tail' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: the periodic-reload row is present" \
          "$(grep -c 'a PERIODIC read must not reload the counter' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: the periodic-pending row is present" \
          "$(grep -c 'a PERIODIC read must not clear pending either' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    #  #474 second wave: eight rows added after a MEASURING seat showed FIVE
    #  one-token mutants sweeping the 24-row detector clean.  All nine mutants now die,
    #  and SIX of them die on a single sole-carrier row -- no row here is redundant:
    #    TIMER_ENABLE   a DISABLED timer can never hold a pending interrupt, so a guard
    #                   widened by it re-opens the defect on the exact state this round
    #                   exists for -- and no other row ever sets that bit
    #    deassert PAIR  the guard wraps THREE effects and the rows observed two.  A row
    #                   wanting ZERO can only fail upward, so the defect row needs its
    #                   ACCEPT companion or a deletion of the effect scores green
    #    anchors        TIMER_1_CLEAR is 0x30C and (0x30C >> 5) & 3 == 0, so every older
    #                   row is an INDEX-0 row.  0x32C and 0x36C pin indices 1 and 3, and
    #                   assert the NEIGHBOUR is untouched so a colliding re-map still reds
    #    index-1 READ   the anchors drive WRITES, so a guard widened per-index passes them
    #                   for the ordinary reason.  This row is the only one carrying BOTH
    #                   properties: which cell, and that the access is refused
    check "footbridge: the deassert ACCEPT row is present" \
          "$(grep -cF '[LIVE] a WRITE DOES deassert the irq' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: the deassert defect row is present" \
          "$(grep -c 'a READ must NOT deassert the irq' "$FBLOG")" "1"
    check "footbridge: the TIMER_ENABLE row is present" \

### `fbenablewidth` (LOW) -- the enable arms at len 8, and the Set / FIQ writes below len 4, are unrowed
#545's floor ends at the five decline arms; the IRQ/FIQ enable arms (+0x188/+0x288) share no case with a decline.  So
a `len == 4` conjunct on their direction tests, on the write-data fetch with the Clear address exempted, or `len != 8`
on the fetch, passes every row (measured, q2_20260923/closing_20260923/CL.log), and IRQEnable's 8-byte read (an LDRD at
+0x188) is not read by any row.  An LDRD/STRD at +0x188 or +0x288 -- or at +0x18c/+0x28c, which the ARM load/store
unit aligns down onto them -- is a guest input.  Disclosed in the contract's NOT COVERED list.  Fix shape: X9/X10
rows for the enable arms at width 8, and the narrow Set/FIQ writes, each with a measured mutant.

### `fbclrdest` (LOW) -- WHICH interrupt a narrow IRQ Clear write drives is unobserved
`INTERRUPT_ASSERT(*(len < 4 ? &d->timer_irq[0] : &d->irq))` in the Clear arm passes all 864 rows (XC_dest,
r3c/r3d tables): X14 counts the CPU line apart from the timer interrupts only at len 4, and X9-X13 count every call
on one shared pair.  Disclosed (the Clear write's body is not an edited line).  OPTIONS: re-wire X9-X13's `d->irq` to
X14's CPU counter pair -- zero new rows, X12/X13's narrow Clears then see the destination (the Opus seat, 3c's pass);
or one len-1 Clear row on X14's split counters (codex, grok, agy, minimax).  Either owes a table: re-wiring changes
what 40 rows observe.

### `fbcallerctx` (LOW) -- every #545 row calls the handler with mem NULL on a machine of type MACHINE_NONE
So a condition on `mem` or on the machine type in any of the five arms' tests passes all 864 rows: the Opus seat's
MC01-MC05 and MC07 (q2f/opus/SUMMARY.txt; green again on 3d, r3d_20260923) -- e.g. `if (writeflag == MEM_READ &&
!mem)` at +0x184 declines every real caller's IRQ_RAW_STATUS read, and a netwinder-only Clear mask leaves ISA line 11
unmaskable.  Clause (e): no body of the five reads `mem` or the machine type.  READY DETECTOR: the Opus seat's scratch
extension (q2f/opus/mkext_q2f.py, diff_ext.c: a cats and a netwinder caller with a real mem, 871 rows, 0 failures on
the fix, 8 of its 9 greens red).

### `fblatchhistory` (LOW) -- a latch that re-arms after more repeats than any row makes passes
`else if (!(d->warned_irq_status_w++ & 3))` announces at the first decline and again at the fifth; no instance
declines one site five times (X6: first and repeat; X11: pairs; P1: four), so it passes all 864 rows (R3D_LH1,
r3d_20260923; codex's dedicated job, 3c's pass).  Clause (e): the latch test partitions {0, nonzero} and both are
rowed; a longer history is unbounded (`& 7` needs nine).  A function-static counter is invisible to any row.

### `fbtimercomments` (records, LOW) -- three pre-existing false sentences in diff_footbridge.c / gate_offline.sh
Filed from #545's passes (R17; codex's dedicated jobs on repair 3's and 3c's passes), not edited in #545 because the
text is the timer campaign's: "EVERY REAL ROW IN THIS FILE COMPARES NUMBERS" (E2, E3, F1 and G1 compare strings); the

pending ticks without testing TIMER_ENABLE, and disabling does not remove the registered timer -- a product question
for the timer campaign as well as a records one.

> pre-fix build, (b) fails on a **VERBATIM REINSTATEMENT of the removed defect at EVERY fixed
> site**, and (c) fails on a null medium (a stale copy, a comment-only file - i.e. it carries
> identity rows). Below the floor, the round's own claim to have "shipped a detector with the
> fix" is a WRONG RECORD, which the stopping rule already fixes in-round.
>
> ### *** CLAUSE (d) - NULL-REPAIR RED. Adopted 2026-08-23 by OWNER RULING, on the round
> that measured its case. ***
>
> **(d) The detector must also fail when the fix is PRESENT but its REPAIR IS NULLED -
> the guard kept, its effect removed.**
>
> **(b) DOES NOT IMPLY (d), and #461 measured both in the same hour on the same detector:**
>
> | mutant | shape | score |
> |---|---|---|
> | `m_dmarevert` | (b): reinstate the `exit(1)` verbatim | **22/31 - killed** |
> | `m_align1` | (d): keep the guard, weaken `& 3` to `& 1` | **27/27 - ESCAPED** |
>
> The detector passed (a), (b) and (c) and was still blind to a ONE-CHARACTER edit that
> re-opened the very defect the round had just closed. (b) restores the DEFECT; (d) neuters
> the FIX. A detector can be sensitive to one and blind to the other, because they fail at
> different sites: (b) fails where the old code was, (d) fails where the new code is.
>
> **HOW TO BUILD THE (d) MUTANT - and it is the same discriminator the accept-row rule
> already uses.** Take each predicate the fix **DEPENDS ON** and weaken it to the
> least-restrictive form that still *reads* like the same check: a mask narrowed
> (`& 3` -> `& 1`), a condition qualified (`if (X)` -> `if (X && writeflag == MEM_WRITE)`),
> a bound relaxed. **Not deletion** - deletion is (b) wearing (d)'s clothes and is usually
> already covered. The test is whether a plausible future edit could produce it.
>
> **WHY THE ONE-CHARACTER FORM IS THE ONE THAT MATTERS.** `dma alias`, the row written
> specifically to pin that repair, was **STRUCTURALLY BLIND** to it: it used an ODD address,
> which both guard forms drop. *The row aimed at the defect could not see the defect return.*
> A row written from the FIX's intent tests the intent, not the boundary - so (d)'s mutant
> has to come from an adversary, which is exactly what the ask-again buys.
>
> **COST: one mutant per repair predicate**, the same order as the accept-row rule (#448 = 4,
> #449 = 1, #461 = 4 across two arms). It is bounded by the diff, so it cannot become #392:
> the predicates are the ones the fix ADDED, and that list is finite and visible.
>
> ### *** "THE PREDICATE THE FIX INTRODUCED" ENUMERATED THE EMPTY SET FOR MOST OF THIS
> ### QUEUE, AND NOBODY NOTICED FOR THREE ROUNDS. Corrected 2026-08-24 -- clause (d) now
> ### reads DEPENDS ON, not INTRODUCED. ***
>
> Clause (d) says *keep the guard, null its repair*. As written it enumerated only
> predicates the diff ADDS -- and **the two commonest fix shapes here add none**:
>
> * a **pure deletion** introduces no predicate at all;
> * a **DE-FATALISATION** introduces none either -- it changes what an EXISTING
>   predicate's branch does. That is most of this project's queue.
>
> So for those rounds clause (d) enumerated **nothing**, and a round could satisfy the
> floor while having built no (d) mutant whatever. `#461` measured (d) successfully only
> because its fix happened to ADD a predicate (an alignment mask) for the mutant to weaken.
>
> **Reading it as DEPENDS ON fixes both cases at once**, and it is why this now replaces a
> narrower deletion-only rule written earlier the same day: that rule was a special case of
> this gap, and two overlapping statements of one rule is the accumulation this carrier is
> supposed to refuse.
>
> **The two shapes, once the enumeration is right:**
> * **fix ADDS a predicate** -> weaken the added one (unchanged; the original rule).
> * **fix adds none** -> the (d) mutant reroutes an EXISTING guard's repair. For a
>   de-fatalisation: **swap the guard's operand for a field the guarded BODY reads.**
>   For a pure deletion: **re-introduce the removed behaviour under a DIFFERENT predicate**
>   -- not the same one widened, which is (b) wearing (d)'s clothes.
>
> *** THE MEASURED CASE, and it is why this matters rather than being tidy: ***
> `#462` de-fatalised two arms. Its guard reads `d->data`; the body it guards reads
> `d->data` AND `d->data_len`; the two are set together in one place and cleared in two
> places that touch only ONE of them. Swapping the guard's operand to `d->data_len == 0`
> **scored a CLEAN SWEEP on a 52-row detector that had just passed twelve mutants**, and
> restored a NULL-pointer read of 408 bytes. Under the old wording that was coverage debt;
> under DEPENDS ON it is a **floor breach, L2, fix in-round** -- which is what the round
> did, before the wording was corrected.
>
> #### *** THE BOUND, and it is not optional: "DEPENDS ON" INVITES TRANSITIVE DATA-FLOW
> #### CHASING, which is unbounded and is how this becomes `#392`. Named 2026-08-24 by the
> #### adjudicating seat, in the same breath as upholding the correction. ***
>
> > **DEPENDS ON enumerates exactly two things: (1) the predicates DOMINATING the diff's
> > edited lines, walked up only to the ENCLOSING FUNCTION BOUNDARY -- never into callees
> > or callers; and (2) for operand-swap forms, swaps drawn ONLY from fields the guarded
> > BODY reads (the guard-field/body-field grep the pre-table check already mandates).**
>
> That is finite, derivable from the diff by reading, and **it provably contains the
> measured case**: the `d->data == NULL` guard dominates the edited decline (clause 1), and
> `data_len` is a body-read field (clause 2), so `M11` falls out mechanically rather than
> by inspiration. It cannot become `#392` because the enumeration stays closed over the
> diff's own hunks -- the same closure the INTRODUCED wording had, without the empty-set
> defect that made INTRODUCED enumerate nothing for a de-fatalisation.
>
> #### *** JOINT CELLS -- adjudicated 2026-09-04 on `pcc2widthkills` pass 10 by the flagship seat, after
> #### a measuring seat's CONJUNCTION of two body-read counts escaped 190/190 AND THE SAME SEAT ARGUED IT
> #### WAS NOT A BREACH.  Both proposed bounds were rejected; the one adopted came from neither. ***
>
> > **DEPENDS-ON enumerates JOINT cells over the guard's operands and the body-read lvalues EXACTLY
> > WHEN THE GUARDED BODY'S OWN CODE DISTINGUISHES THEM.**  For each lvalue take the PARTITION the
> > body's own tests induce (`pending > 0` gives {0, >0}; `relative_addr == PCCTWO_T1ICR` gives
> > {T1, T2}); the enumerated cells are the CROSS-PRODUCT of those partitions -- never arbitrary
> > boolean functions of the read-set.  A mutant exempting a single cell whose reachability is
> > MEASURED is a floor breach; close the whole cross-product in ONE diff and write into the
> > contract which cells are rowed.  A value or combination the body never tests as a case
> > (`idata == k`; a byte no conditional distinguishes; both counts >= 2 where the body tests `> 0`)
> > is DISCLOSED under clause (e).
>
> **Why this form and not the two that were argued.**  The dissent's discriminator -- *a plausible
> future edit* -- failed three ways: plausibility is not derivable by reading; the mutant was written
> and measured, so it is an edit someone writes; and "single-operand is complete" is measurably
> false, since `&& idata != 2` is single-operand and escapes.  The main loop's own bound -- *pairs of
> body-read lvalues over a finite reachable space* -- was rejected as SELF-REFUTING: the counts' raw
> value space is 2^32 apiece, so "finite" does no bounding work on its motivating case (a C `int` is
> finite -- the first wording here said it was not; codex and glm caught it on pass 11); "reachable
> guest state" would force rowing
> `idata == 2` that the same record discloses; and a PAIRS cap misses `#499`'s four axes.  The
> body-tested-partition form is finite (a product of small partition counts), derivable from the
> diff by reading the body's own `if`s, and it enumerates `#499`'s width x component x direction x
> position cells from `len`, `writeflag` and `relative_addr` -- which neither argued bound reached.
>
> **The measured case.**  The TxICR body tests both pending counts at `> 0` and the label at
> `== T1ICR` / `T2ICR`, so the closure is {T2, T1} x {p1 = 0, > 0} x {p2 = 0, > 0} -- eight cells.
> Pass 9 held one open cell (both counts, T2 label: a 190/190 escape, predicted by the Opus seat
> before its build); pass 10 found the MIRROR (both counts, T1 label) and the (p2 only, T1 label)
> cell open -- the `#499` shape reproduced inside the repair for it -- and the label x p1 x p2 x IEN
> product (sixteen cells) closed in one diff of nine readbacks in existing sessions; the ICLR, INT and
> 0xe0 partitions were disclosed by count, and pass 11 then found the ICLR x pending cell open and
> in-rule (this paragraph said "eight cells ... three readbacks" until pass 11).  **The De Morgan form of the gap**, from the kimi
> substitute: `!(p1 && p2)` is `p1 == 0 || p2 == 0`; neither disjunct is true of all its lvalue's
> held values, the disjunction is true in every row, so a per-lvalue disclosure test can never fire
> on it.  That is why the per-lvalue value-domain statement, correct as far as it went, could not
> see this class at all.
>
> **Two corollaries from the same pass, both measured.**  The PRE-TABLE CHECK's SIBLING PARITY row
> compares BLOCKS; at a block that serves TWO case labels the siblings are the LABELS, and a
> parity census over the row names is what would have shown `pendboth` as the only T2-only cell
> before its table ran (Opus, pass 10).  **AND THE JOIN ITSELF, adjudicated 2026-09-09 on `sgimtedepth4dead` 7g-1 after a MEASURED total escape: a dispatch LABEL is a floor axis of an edited guard when the selector's value FLOWS INTO an lvalue the guarded body reads or writes within the enclosing function (there, `dst_mode = (dst_tlb << 10) + ...` two lines above a repair of `dst_mode |= RGBA`), or when the body re-tests it after the join (TxICR); a label that reaches the edit and touches nothing the body reads is clause (e) (the `mode & COPY` shape).  A detector rowing one label of three was swept 14/14 by `&& dst_tlb == MTE_TLB_A` while a guest-only control showed the closed defect live at the other two (`regress/artifacts/sgimtedepth4dead/killtable3_20260909/`).  The floor it adds is {labels} x the guard operand's body-tested partition -- six cells there, two rows owed -- never the labels' cross-product with everything.**  And the measuring seat's own bound -- the cross-product
> of the value-CLASSES the rows establish, with the dispatch axes as full axes, demand
> INCREMENTAL -- agrees with the body-tested form on every cell that mattered here and differs on
> where the classes come from (the rows, or the body's tests); that residual difference is
> carried to the next pass as a named question rather than settled by the author.
>
> #### *** THE LETTER OVER-DEMANDED, AND ITS OWN AUTHOR BOUNDED IT ONE PASS LATER (pass 11, 2026-09-04). ***
>
> Read literally, "the cross-product of the body-tested partitions" demanded ALL 128 cells at the TxICR
> arm -- the body also tests `idata & IEN`, `idata & ICLR`, the register's INT bit and `idata & 0xe0` --
> and it compounds as 2^k in the `if`-count across twelve arms: the `#392` shape wearing the rule's
> clothes.  Three seats said the letter demands the remainder; the flagship seat, asked to be harder on
> its own rule than on anyone, ruled AGAINST the letter:
>
> > **The rule ENUMERATES joint cells wherever the body distinguishes them; the FLOOR -- the cells a row
> > is OWED for -- is the cross-product of the guard's dominating predicates with the device-STATE lvalues
> > the fix's own repair path reads that are JOINTLY REACHABLE through its own data flow.**  The bits of
> > the single value being written are single-operand MARGINALS, not floor; joint cells outside the floor
> > are clause-(e) coverage debt, subject to the one ask-again.  A filter may reject or discharge a cell
> > but may demand a new row only for a floor cell -- so it cannot grow unbounded.
>
> At TxICR the floor is {label} x {p1} x {p2}: one `pcc_timer_tick` callback increments both counts and
> `reassert_interrupts` reads both, so the pair is a joint input of the model.  Eight cells, all rowed.
>
> #### *** THE FLOOR INCLUDES THE SINGLE-AXIS GENERATING SET, AND "DERIVED" STATE IS NOT DISPOSABLE.
> #### Adjudicated 2026-09-04 by the fix review (Fable 5.1 adjudicating; GPT-6 Astra at max dissenting on the
> #### bound and supplying the two findings that made the amendment measurable). ***
>
> > **At each floor cell, every idata bit the guarded body TESTS is written both clear and set.**  The bare
> > state floor above does not catch `M_ICLR` -- an idata-bit x state joint -- and would grade the pass-11
> > escape as debt while the CHANGELOG grades it L2; the generating set is what makes the two consistent.
> > Multi-axis idata joints stay clause-(e) debt.  **The codex seat adopts the Opus strength-t covering
> > array instead** (its `t` = the body's longest conjunction) and is RECORDED, not adopted -- for COST (seven
> > binary axes at TxICR make a strength-4 array tens of pty cells per arm: the 2^k growth by another door) and
> > because a covering array certifies that a tuple OCCURS, not that an observable differs, so it cannot be the
> > floor without the CHANNEL obligation doing the work.  *This clause first said "`t` is a judgment input";
> > the array's own author refuted that on pass 13 -- `t` is read off the body -- and supplied the two grounds.*
>
> **The two measured consequences, both found by a seat reading the ENUMERATION against the handler rather
> than the tables against the enumeration:**
>

>
> **THE STOP CONDITION: one pass per floor-bound diff, closing on the FIRST PASS THAT FINDS
> ZERO BREACHES** -- subject to the two corrections below, without which it closes early on a
> live escape.
>
> #### CORRECTION 1 -- A ZERO NEEDS A VALIDITY CONDITION ON THE PASS, AND A DENOMINATOR
>
> As first written, "zero breaches" counted a pass's OUTPUT with no condition on the pass
> itself. **Three briefs that day went out with the diff missing** -- a `git show | head -N`
> inlined the commit message instead -- and a panel answering "no breaches" against an empty
> packet would have CLOSED THE CHAIN.
>
> > **A closing pass must be a VALID pass: the diff actually delivered; the roster complete or
> > its misses named per the seat ledger; and the zero carried WITH ITS DENOMINATOR -- which
> > cells, which mutant shapes, were swept.** *"Zero found"* and *"zero looked for"* must not
> > render the same, which is this project's oldest rule about blanks turned on its own stop
> > condition.
>
> That also converts the rule's finishing incentive into a checkable artifact: **a bare zero
> does not close; a zero with an enumeration does.**
>
> #### CORRECTION 2 -- SCOPE: EFFORT ON THE DIFF, THE FLOOR QUESTION ON THE COMPOSITE
>
> *"Scoped to that diff"* is ambiguous and **the dangerous reading would have closed `#499`
> over a live total escape.** The breach that pass 3 found was not in that pass's two hunks --
> the hunks were fine. It lived in the SHIPPED COMPOSITE detector's coverage of the ORIGINAL
> fix's mutant space.
>
> > **Review EFFORT is scoped to the repair diff, so settled design is not re-litigated. The
> > FLOOR QUESTION -- does a clean-sweep mutant exist in the (d) enumeration -- is asked of the
> > SHIPPED COMPOSITE on every pass.**
>
> #### THE BOUND IS IMPORTED, NOT INTRINSIC -- state the dependency or the rule overclaims
>
> The stop condition does not bound the chain and must not pretend to. The bound comes from
> **the (d) DEPENDS-ON enumeration being diff-closed and finite**, and from *zero breaches*
> meaning **zero SURVIVING ADJUDICATION** rather than zero raised -- otherwise one stubborn
> seat blocks closure forever, against the count-arguments-not-seats rule. With those imports
> the chain is infinite only if every repair is defective, in which case every pass is buying a
> real defect off the ship.
>
> #### *** THE AMENDMENT THAT ACTUALLY ENDS CHAINS, and it is the part worth carrying. ***
>
> `#499`'s three breaches were **THE SAME CLASS THREE TIMES** -- a cell of one state space left
> empty, repaired at exactly the address the mutant had used, leaving the symmetric cell open
> for the next pass. Pass 1 found *width* unpinned; pass 2 found *component*; pass 3 found
> *direction* AND *position*. **Pass-per-instance converges LINEARLY IN CELLS**, and 28 -> 34
> -> 36 rows bought one cell each.
>
> > **WHEN TWO CONSECUTIVE PASSES FIND BREACHES OF ONE CLASS, THE NEXT REPAIR OWES THE CLASS
> > CLOSURE: enumerate the full space from the function's own inputs, close every cell in ONE
> > diff, and write into the CONTRACT which cells are covered and which are deliberately not.

> ### OPEN -- filed 2026-09-11 from `#524` pass D: a cheap way to get more assertions per session
> ### silently moves every assertion after the first onto the FAST path
>
> **`multiaccesswarms`** (MED) -- **a probe row testing a SLOW-PATH fix with a MULTI-ACCESS
> instruction is vacuous from the second access onward.**  The first cold access goes through the
> slow path *and warms the page*; every later access in that session takes the FAST path, which the
> fix does not touch and a slow-path mutant does not alter.
>
> Measured on `ARM_LDM_MULTI_P2_R2`: a mutant that deviates at the second access left the row GREEN.
> Found by kimi (measuring); glm reached it from the other end by calling the kill table
> *"internally impossible as stated"* because a row it expected to red did not.
>
> **This is the CHANNEL check's own shape** -- *a row whose named channel cannot vary under its
> named mutant is vacuous by construction* -- **and it is knowable at WRITING time.**  It was missed
> by reasoning about the ARITHMETIC of the second access, which is genuinely different, instead of
> about WHICH PATH SERVICES IT.

**2. Q2 — A one-expression candidate escapes E4’s endpoint observation.**

- **“Five X14 checks” is miscounted.** E1 changes two named rows, E2 one, E3 two and E4 one: **six named rows across four `check_u` call sites**. Both the X14 recap and IDENTITY comment need consistent wording.
- **“As a real store’s [bytes above the width] are not zero” is false.** The ARM caller uses `data[1]` and `data[2]` for narrow stores; bytes beyond those arrays are not valid store data. Describe `0xa5` as deliberately poisoned padding that detects this fetch-width error.
- **The E3 rationale is too broad.** Requiring one after the deasserting Clear would preserve today’s defect. Requiring one after the asserting Clear would not.
- **The LEVEL disclosure needs FS02’s condition.** “A Clear that clears irq_asserted” overstates what remains invisible after E4.

- Clear `0x400`: enable `0x10`, raw status `0x10`, one CPU assert.
